Running evidence log

Is AI creating new demand, or just moving revenue around?

Verified evidence that non-software corporations are getting top-line revenue impact from AI spend — sorted into three shapes. A second, separately counted cost ledger tracks AI-attributed cost savings on a 2×2 of sector × function, because that evidence arrives far more regularly and must not be allowed to stand in for the revenue question. Updated daily at 07:00 PT.

S3 = Demand that did not previously exist. GDP-additive, not share-shifting. S2 = Revenue on existing products in existing segments. Zero-sum at industry level. S1 = Faster or higher-capacity inputs to revenue. A leading indicator, not realized ROI.

2026-08-22S2Carnegie Mellon University / Larridin - 'Do AI Adoption Signals Predict Company Performance?' (564 companies, 538 after dedup, 478 10-K filings, 30,000+ job postings)Cross-sector (research)Cross-sectional study surfaced 17 Aug 2026 testing whether public AI-adoption signals - vendor tracker scores, AI hiring intensity, investment intensity and 10-K disclosure language - predict revenue growth, margin improvement or stock returns across 538 companies in 12 sectors. Only one signal survived: narrative concreteness, where firms at the top of the distribution are associated with 8.0 percentage points higher year-over-year revenue growth than those at the bottom (coefficient +0.080, p=0.009). Broader adoption measures weakened under multiple-testing correction, adoption showed little connection to margins, and no public AI signal predicted four-month forward stock returns. AI infrastructure suppliers beat sector- and size-matched peers by roughly 32 points over four months.DISCLOSEDClow

2026-08-15S2OpenAI / Chatterji, Holtz, Rakholia, Tambe & Weeratunga - 'How Organizations Use AI: Evidence from ChatGPT' (1,500+ organizations, 17m+ messages, 417 US public-company tickers)Cross-sector (research)Working paper submitted 12 Aug 2026 linking ChatGPT Enterprise account records to Compustat financials for US public companies through March 2026. On the only revenue question it asks, the answer is null: "Revenue per employee is not meaningfully associated with output tokens per employee once other controls are included", and for the other usage margins "the point estimates are positive for messages per active usage week per employee and weekly active users per employee, but neither coefficient is statistically significant." Adoption runs the other way - "firms with higher revenue per employee are more likely to adopt ChatGPT Enterprise", with "a one-log-point increase in lagged revenue per employee ... associated with roughly 0.4 to 0.9 percentage points higher adoption probability."DISCLOSEDCmedium

2026-08-02C4BIS Working Paper 1325 - 'AI adoption, productivity and employment: evidence from European firms' (12,000+ non-financial EU/US firms)Cross-sector (research)Instrumental-variables study of matched EIBIS-ORBIS data on "more than 12,000 non-financial firms in the European Union (EU) and United States (US)", instrumenting EU firms' AI adoption with the adoption rates of matched US peers: "AI adoption increases the level of labor productivity by 4%." 2026-08-29

permalink →

Assessment

No S3. Three findings, and the most useful is the absence: a systematic sweep of seven P&C insurers' Q2 2026 calls found every one raised AI unprompted and not one isolated its economic effect.

Sources read

  • FactSet Insight (14 Aug, Stewart Johnson) - seven insurers, one quarter, one number: Travelers' 0.5-point Business Insurance underlying loss-ratio improvement, which AI 'contributed to' with the discrete share undisclosed. Chubb's CEO said token costs are 'a minor fraction' of gains and that Chubb tracks it in hard dollars internally, then shared none. Hamilton, AIG, MetLife, Selective and American Financial Group attributed no ratio move at all.
  • CIBC Q3 2026 call (27 Aug) - AdvisorAssist cuts adviser administrative time 'by up to 50%'; AI 2.0 billed as the first enterprise-wide agentic workspace in Canadian banking. No net-new-assets or revenue figure anywhere in the transcript.
  • TD Bank Group Q3 2026 call (27 Aug) - C$195m of 'value from AI' three quarters into FY26 against a C$200m target, medium-term ambition C$1bn split evenly between revenue uplift and cost savings. TD never says which half the C$195m sits in, and none of these figures appear in the filed results release.
  • Best Buy Q2 FY27 call (27 Aug) - 'emerging categories' including AI glasses more than doubled year over year, bundled with trading cards and health rings.
  • Travelers Q2 2026 call coverage - fetched separately to confirm the 0.5-point figure and its framing rather than take FactSet's rendering on trust.
The pattern across five weeks now: the disclosure gap is sectoral, not company-specific. Insurers and banks will describe the capability in detail and will not put a number beside it - and when they do put a number beside it, as TD has, the number is a construct the company declines to define.

Worth separating two failure modes that look alike. CIBC discloses a real input metric and simply never connects it to revenue. TD discloses a headline dollar figure that cannot be assigned to either ledger because 'value' is undefined. The second is the more corrosive habit, because it produces a scoreboard number that reads like a P&L effect.

Near-misses deliberately not logged

  • S3 insurance, new risk classes - checked carrier appetite commentary and the AI-liability market. The traffic runs the wrong way: AIG, Great American and W.R. Berkley filed to exclude AI-related liabilities, so the most-cited S3 hunting ground in insurance is currently contracting, not opening. Failed the inclusion bar anyway - no named carrier statement with a figure.
  • S2 autos, ADAS subscriptions - GM disclosed $6.3bn OnStar deferred revenue and $800m recognized in Q2, but management attributed none of it to AI; the AI framing is the trade press's. Ford's BlueCruise figures are already logged from 2026-08-01 and were only restated. Failed attribution and dedupe respectively.
  • S3 retail, AI-enabled SKU - Best Buy sells AI glasses and integrated commerce into ChatGPT, but the doubling is a bundled category with trading cards and health rings, and retailing someone else's AI device is AI-as-product-sold, not ROI on AI deployed. Failed the isolation test and the as-capability test.
  • S1/C4 banks - TD Insurance vehicle damage estimation and Sona Mehta's 'first resi agentic AI capability' both lack any number; folded into the TD entry as context rather than logged separately.
  • S2 surveys - Deloitte and Futurum cuts reporting that roughly 20% of organizations already grow revenue from AI while 74% aspire to. Consultant sentiment, and substantively the same claim as the McKinsey entry logged 2026-08-28. Failed the bar and the dedupe rule.
  • S3 professional services and media localization - trade-press claims that AI makes advisory work and dubbing viable at new price points are real in mechanism but had no named firm attaching revenue to them. Failed the named-company test.

Nearest dated catalysts

  • Travelers Q3 2026 call in mid-October - the single best test of the FactSet finding, since Innovation 2.0 and the Travis quoting platform give management an obvious place to put a number.
  • TD Q4 and FY2026 results in early December - whether the C$200m is finally split into revenue uplift and cost savings.
  • CIBC Q4 2026 in early December - whether AdvisorAssist appears with any wealth-segment metric attached.
  • Mid-October earnings cluster generally: the last two runs of thin retail and bank disclosure are consistent with an off-week, not with a change in the underlying rate.
Seven P&C insurers reviewed together - Travelers, Chubb, MetLife, American Financial Group, Selective, AIG, Hamilton Insurance Group Insurance · US / Bermuda S1 — Velocity⚠ Counter-evidence

A FactSet Insight review (14 Aug 2026) of seven insurers' Q2 2026 earnings calls finds all seven raised AI unprompted and none isolated its economic effect. The only figure attached to AI at all is Travelers' 0.5-point improvement in the Business Insurance underlying loss ratio, which management said AI and innovation investments 'contributed to' without disclosing AI's discrete share. Chubb's CEO said token costs are 'a fraction, a minor fraction' of the gains and that Chubb tracks the comparison in hard dollars internally, but shared no dollar or basis-point figure. Hamilton called AI 'a productivity and intelligence multiplier' with no productivity, ratio or volume figures attached; American Financial Group cited 'encouraging productivity improvements, particularly in claims' with no figures; AIG, MetLife and Selective attributed no ratio move.

Mechanism
Nothing is being measured here except the disclosure itself. The claimed mechanisms - better risk selection, straight-through claims processing, submission triage - are the same ones this log has recorded from Aviva, Skyward, Ryan Specialty and Baldwin. What is new is a systematic cross-carrier check of whether any of them convert into a disclosed number, run across a full quarter of one sector's calls.
Why this shape
Counter-evidence tagged to S1, which is the shape almost every insurance AI disclosure in this log has taken: an input-metric improvement with no revenue conversion. The finding is that the silence is not carrier-specific but sector-wide. Seven carriers, one quarter, one partial loss-ratio figure that is explicitly not attributed to AI alone - and no carrier put AI next to premium, hit ratio, or new business at all. Note the direction of the residual claim too: even the one number is a loss-ratio (cost/underwriting) figure, not a top-line one, so the sector's own best case for AI is currently a margin story it will not size.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier C — Third-party reporting — journalism, trade press, sell-side
FactSet Insight - 'Insurance: AI Investments Emerge (So Far) as Qualitative Comments vs Quantitative Drivers', Stewart Johnson, 2026-08-14
Falsifier
A Q3 or Q4 2026 call from any of these seven attaching a discrete dollar or basis-point figure to AI - or, stronger, to bound premium or hit ratio - would break the pattern. Travelers' 'Innovation 2.0' and the Travis submission platform are the most likely place for it to appear first.
Confidence
medium — Tier C secondary reading of Tier A transcripts; the load-bearing Travelers figure was independently confirmed this run against Q2 call coverage, the other six were not re-checked line by line.
Canadian Imperial Bank of Commerce (CIBC) Banking · Canada S1 — Velocity

On the Q3 2026 earnings call (27 Aug 2026) CEO Harry Culham said CIBC launched AdvisorAssist, 'an AI-enabled platform that helps advisers spend more time with clients. The system automates meeting notes, summaries and follow-up documentation while supporting regulatory compliance, and this reduces administrative time for advisers by up to 50%.' Culham also announced 'CIBC AI 2.0, the first enterprise-wide agentic AI workspace in Canadian banking.' No revenue, net-new-assets, or client-acquisition figure was attached to either.

Mechanism
Advisers in wealth and private banking spend a large fraction of the working week on meeting documentation and compliance write-ups. Automating that returns hours to the client-facing calendar, which is the input to advice sales and asset gathering. The revenue path runs through adviser capacity, not through any change in what CIBC sells.
Why this shape
S1 on the METHOD test: the disclosed figure is an input metric (adviser time), and the process it feeds - adviser client coverage - is revenue-producing. Revenue conversion was conspicuously absent: nothing in the transcript links AI to net new assets, wealth revenue or client acquisition, and the CFO's only AI reference was to 'AI-enabled productivity across our bank.' The same figure has a defensible C4 reading as back-office cost; logged once, as S1, because the disclosure's stated purpose is adviser client capacity rather than expense. Note also that 'up to 50%' is a ceiling, not an average - the honest read is that CIBC has disclosed the best case for one workflow.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier A — Primary and accountable — filing, transcript, formal guidance
CIBC Q3 2026 earnings call transcript, 2026-08-27
Falsifier
If CIBC's wealth segment reports through FY2027 without any adviser-productivity, net-new-asset or revenue-per-adviser metric tied to AdvisorAssist, the 50% figure was a launch statistic rather than a capacity gain that reached the top line.
Confidence
low — Tier A quote verified verbatim this run, but 'up to 50%' on one workflow with no revenue conversion is the weakest form of S1 this log accepts.
Cost ledger — AI-attributed cost savings and productivity gains, on a 2×2 of sector (tech / non-tech) × function (software engineering / everything else). Tech subjects are in scope here, unlike the revenue log. A separate ledger: never counted in the S1–S3 revenue totals, because cost evidence is far more abundant than revenue evidence and pooling the two would bury the question this log exists to answer.
TD Bank Group Banking · Canada C4 — Non-tech · non-engineering

On the Q3 2026 earnings call (27 Aug 2026) CEO Raymond Chun said: 'Three quarters into the year, we have essentially hit our fiscal 2026 target of CAD 200 million in value from AI', with C$195 million delivered through three quarters. TD's stated medium-term ambition is C$1 billion of AI value, split as C$500 million of annualized revenue uplift and C$500 million of annualized cost savings. The three named transformation areas are retail end-to-end credit, the software development cycle and contact centres. Group Head Sona Mehta said TD 'fully scaled our first resi agentic AI capability, and it's absolutely delivering faster decisions.'

Mechanism
Document review and decisioning in retail credit, code generation in the software development cycle, and call automation in contact centres. TD Insurance additionally runs AI-powered vehicle damage estimation on claims.
Why this shape
Logged in the cost ledger, and specifically as an instance of METHOD's composite-value trap. 'Value from AI' is a company-defined construct: TD's own medium-term framing splits C$1 billion evenly between revenue uplift and cost savings, which means the realized C$195 million is by construction neither a disclosed cost reduction nor a disclosed revenue gain, and TD did not break it out. Placed in C4 because contact centres and retail credit operations dominate the named use cases, but one of the three - the software development cycle - properly belongs in C3, and TD does not size the cells either. One further tell worth recording: none of these figures appear in TD's own Q3 2026 results release, which mentions AI only as 'investing in front-line talent, AI and innovation to deepen client relationships.' The number lives in oral remarks, not in the filed document.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier A — Primary and accountable — filing, transcript, formal guidance
TD Bank Group Q3 2026 earnings call transcript, 2026-08-27
Falsifier
TD should be able to state, by the FY2026 close in Q4, how much of the C$200 million is revenue uplift versus cost savings, since its own medium-term target is stated in exactly those two halves. If the FY2026 number is reported as another undifferentiated 'value' figure, or if the medium-term C$1 billion stops being split, treat the construct as a scoreboard rather than a P&L effect.
Confidence
medium — Tier A quote confirmed against two renderings fetched this run (transcript and PYMNTS earnings coverage); the deduction is capped by TD never defining 'value'.

2026-08-28

permalink →

Assessment

No S3 today, and no revenue-attributed finding of any shape. The day's substantive item is counter-evidence: McKinsey's 2026 wave finds the share of firms attributing any EBIT impact to AI flat year over year, on 1,719 respondents.

Sources read

  • McKinsey State of AI 2026, via The Register (25 Aug) - 37% attribute at least some EBIT impact to AI, "about the same" as 2025; 6% qualify as high performers, "flat since last year"; 20% say AI operating costs constrain use. McKinsey's own page returned 503 twice, and renderings disagree on the headline (37% vs 39%), so only the retrieved figure is logged.
  • Royal Bank of Canada, Q3 2026 call (27 Aug) - McKay reaffirmed CAD 700m-1bn of AI "enterprise value" by end of fiscal 2027. RBC defines that as run-rate revenue, cost avoidance, expense savings, fraud reduction and lower cost of risk, net of investments. Composite, undecomposed, so it cannot enter the revenue log.
  • Dollar General, Q2 2026 call (27 Aug) - Vasos is "building agentic operating systems for the enterprise"; Lau says the long-term framework "doesn't contemplate any potential benefit from AI". No number either way.
  • Best Buy, Q2 FY2027 call (27 Aug) - Ask Blue assistant in phased rollout, OpenAI commerce integration completed. Neither carries a disclosed number.
  • Deere, Q3 FY2026 8-K (20 Aug) - the release's only mention of AI sits in the forward-looking-statements risk block.
  • Ulta Beauty, Dick's Sporting Goods, CIBC, IAG, SK Telecom - checked and rejected, reasons below.
The cross-run pattern hardens. Five weeks in, the non-tech disclosures that clear the bar are overwhelmingly S1 input metrics whose revenue conversion is never reported, and today two independent readings of the same fact arrived on the same tape: an n=1,719 survey that cannot find enterprise EBIT impact, and a $40bn retailer whose CFO models exactly zero of it.

Near-misses deliberately not logged

  • S3 insurance, new risk classes - parametric market reports, Aon and broker material, New India Assurance product launch. Failed the inclusion bar: no named deployer with a bound-premium number; all vendor or trade-body framing of what is now possible.
  • S3 pharma, unscreenable target space - ASCO 2026 count of 117 AI-enabled assets across 63 companies, 8 past Phase 2, zero approvals. Failed the named-company and primary-source tests: MDPI returned 403, PharmaLetter returned empty, and the figures survive only in aggregator renderings. Directionally strong counter-evidence and worth re-fetching next run.
  • S3 healthcare, new price points - CPT-2026 adds 288 new codes including AI-assisted diagnostic and short-window remote monitoring categories. Failed the result test: a reimbursement pathway is a precondition for S3, not evidence of it. No provider has yet disclosed revenue billed against the new codes.
  • S3 industrials, autonomy as a new line - Deere, Trimble, Caterpillar. Failed the capability-gate and disclosure tests: Deere's Q3 strength is Construction & Forestry up 18% on data-centre build-out, which is AI-as-customer on pre-existing SKUs, and the release attributes nothing to AI.
  • S3 professional services - BCG's "roughly 40% of revenue AI-tied by 2026". Failed the subject-scope test and the counterfactual test: a consultancy selling AI services is out of scope as a subject, and the work displaces other consulting spend rather than creating new demand.
  • S3 SMB long-tail - HubSpot Breeze at a $20/seat Starter tier, IDC adoption data. Failed the subject-scope and evidence tests: software vendor as subject, and no revenue disclosed for the tier.
  • S2 telecom - SK Telecom Q2 2026, AI revenue of KRW 197.5bn, of which KRW 136.2bn is data centre and GPUaaS up 92.5%. Failed the AI-as-capability test: capacity sold to AI buyers, not AI deployed against SKT's own revenue. The KRW 61.3bn B2B/B2C line is cloud resale orders, same problem.
  • S2 retail late-August - Ulta Beauty (8.9% net sales growth, 47m loyalty members) and Dick's Sporting Goods made no AI or machine-learning reference at all on their calls. Best Buy's emerging-category strength, including AI glasses reportedly more than doubling, is a new consumer category retailed, not ROI on Best Buy's own AI spend - the shelf-level version of the genset trade.
  • S2 banks - CIBC launched "CIBC AI 2.0" on its Q3 call (27 Aug) with no figure attached. IAG FY2026 disclosed >60% of employees using AI and an OpenAI partnership - an adoption metric, METHOD's trap 1, with no premium or cost number.
  • S1 agentic commerce - Adobe and third-party trackers on AI-referred traffic and conversion. Failed the named-company and denominator tests: aggregate traffic multiples are not attributed incremental revenue at any named deployer.
  • Counter-evidence, walk-backs - C3.ai's Q1 miss (revenue ~33% below guidance midpoint) is a software vendor's own sales, out of scope as a subject and not adoption evidence.

Nearest dated catalysts

  • 3 Sep - Lululemon Q2, the last major apparel read of the cycle.
  • Early-to-mid Sep - Costco, Oracle, Adobe; Kroger and the grocery cohort follow.
  • Q4 - Dollar General's loyalty and subscription pilot launches "end of year", with full rollout in 2027; the first checkpoint on whether Lau's zero-AI framework survives contact.
  • Oct 2027 - end of RBC's fiscal 2027 and the terminal date on the CAD 700m-1bn AI target. Interim checkpoints at each quarterly call; watch for a restated definition.
  • Mid-Oct - Q3 earnings season proper, the next dense cluster of non-tech AI attribution.
McKinsey State of AI 2026 global survey (1,719 respondents) Cross-sector · Global S2 — Share⚠ Counter-evidence

McKinsey's 2026 State of AI wave, reported 25 Aug 2026, finds 37% of respondents "attribute at least some EBIT impact to AI use" - "about the same" share as the 2025 survey - and only 6% qualify as "AI high performers" (at least 5% of EBIT attributed to AI plus a self-described "significant" impact), a figure that "remained flat since last year". 20% cited AI-related operating costs as constraining their use of the technology.

Mechanism
None - this entry measures the absence of a mechanism. The survey asks executives to attribute enterprise-level EBIT to AI use; the finding is that the share able to do so did not move over twelve months, and that among those who can, most put the figure below 5% of EBIT.
Why this shape
Counter-evidence tagged to S2, the shape that claims firm-level attributed financial impact on existing business. It contradicts the proposition that AI deployment is now producing bankable P&L effect at scale: on a 1,719-respondent base the attribution rate is flat year over year while capex is not. Logged despite METHOD's standing rejection of consultant surveys because the direction of the finding runs against the surveyor's commercial interest - selection bias in a McKinsey AI survey pushes toward overstating impact, so a null result is the one reading a consultant survey is well positioned to deliver. Not a revenue finding and not counted as one.
Status
ESTIMATED — company's own internal attribution
Source
Tier D — Vendor case study or consultant survey — lowest tier
The Register report on McKinsey State of AI 2026 global survey (n=1,719), 2026-08-25
Falsifier
The 2027 wave shows the any-EBIT-impact share and the 6% high-performer share materially higher on a comparable base. If both are still flat in a third consecutive year while AI capex compounds, the enterprise-adoption S-curve story needs rewriting, not restating.
Confidence
low — McKinsey's own page returned HTTP 503 on two attempts this run, so the figures come from a Tier C rendering; secondary renderings disagree on the headline (37% in the document retrieved, 39% in a search summary of McKinsey's page), and only the retrieved figure is recorded here. Self-reported attribution, not audited.
Cost ledger — AI-attributed cost savings and productivity gains, on a 2×2 of sector (tech / non-tech) × function (software engineering / everything else). Tech subjects are in scope here, unlike the revenue log. A separate ledger: never counted in the S1–S3 revenue totals, because cost evidence is far more abundant than revenue evidence and pooling the two would bury the question this log exists to answer.
Royal Bank of Canada Banking · Canada C4 — Non-tech · non-engineering

On the Q3 2026 earnings call (27 Aug 2026) CEO Dave McKay said RBC is "accelerating the execution around our AI ambitions as we build towards generating CAD 700 million to CAD 1 billion in enterprise value by the end of fiscal 2027". RBC has previously defined that enterprise value as "the annualized exit run-rate benefit in revenue, cost avoidance, expense savings, fraud reduction and lower cost of risk, net of investments", incremental from fiscal 2024.

Mechanism
A dedicated AI Group reporting to the CEO, built on Borealis AI, an NVIDIA partnership and proprietary LLMs. Named workstreams on the call: advisor productivity tools in wealth management, back-office streamlining, and "AI for Alpha" across "30 plus customized investment processes". No workstream carries its own disclosed number.
Why this shape
Cost ledger, C4 - a non-tech incumbent, function is everything-but-engineering. It is not a revenue entry and must not be read as one: the target is a composite of five different things (revenue, cost avoidance, expense savings, fraud reduction, lower cost of risk) with no split published, which is METHOD's standing trap 3 exactly. RBC deserves credit for defining the composite at all - most issuers of a "value" number will not - but an undecomposed number cannot answer whether any of it is top line. The date-stamped, net-of-investment framing is the useful part: it is falsifiable, which almost no AI target is.
Status
PROJECTED — forward target, not a result
Source

RBC Q3 2026 earnings call (transcript rendering); target definition from PYMNTS, 3 Dec 2025, 2026-08-27
Falsifier
RBC's FY2027 results (Dec 2027) do not report a realized run-rate figure against the CAD 700m-1bn, report it materially below CAD 700m, or drop the metric from disclosure. A restatement of the definition mid-flight - especially one that widens what counts as value - is the same failure in slower motion.
Confidence
medium — The target and its definition are corroborated by two independent retrieved sources, but the specific 27 Aug wording comes from one third-party transcript rendering; a second rendering of the same call (MarketBeat) did not carry the figure. Target is forward-looking with no interim realized number disclosed.
Dollar General Retail - discount · US C4 — Non-tech · non-engineering⚠ Counter-evidence

On the Q2 2026 call (27 Aug 2026) CEO Todd Vasos said Dollar General is "building agentic operating systems for the enterprise, focused on reshaping and optimizing our workflows to improve productivity throughout the organization" and cited "increased use of artificial intelligence" among efficiency drivers - while CFO Donny Lau said "the long-term framework doesn't contemplate any potential benefit from AI, and we're still early days here". No AI figure was given on either the cost or the revenue side.

Mechanism
Agentic workflow automation across enterprise processes, described at the level of ambition rather than deployment. The quantified operating detail on the call sat with delivery and the DG Media Network, neither of which was attributed to AI.
Why this shape
Cost ledger, C4, and counter-evidence: METHOD's trap 2 - productivity management explicitly declines to bank - in its purest form so far, and stronger than the Amex precedent because Lau excludes AI from the multi-year framework outright rather than reinvesting a claimed gain. An `$11.3bn`-quarter retailer publicly building agentic systems while its CFO models zero benefit is the AI-capex-versus-realized-P&L gap stated by the party with the most reason to claim otherwise. Nothing here is a revenue shape; there is no revenue path disclosed, absent or implied.

Assessment

Two entries, both from Williams-Sonoma, and no S3 for the eleventh consecutive run. The retail agentic cluster gained its most extreme ratios yet and still not one dollar of attributed revenue.

Sources read

  • Williams-Sonoma (Q2 FY2026 call, 26 Aug) - Olive engagement +700%, revenue +620%, engagers converting at 3x; personalized visits at 9x the revenue of an average visit, up from 2x. Total revenue $1.96bn, comps +6.2%, none of it AI-attributed. Logged S1.

  • Abercrombie & Fitch (Q2, 26 Aug) - record net sales $1.27bn, +5%, fifteenth straight growth quarter. CEO Fran Horowitz named AI twice, both times as "speed and efficiency" with no number attached. Not logged.
  • CIBC (Q3 FY26, 27 Aug) - net income $2.41bn, revenue $8.37bn. The release names CAI 2.0, AdvisorAssist and Knowledge Central. Every one is a pilot or a launch with zero quantification. Fails the result bar outright.

The EDGAR 6-K I actually retrieved was the Q2 filing, not Q3; its single AI reference sits in the forward-looking-statements risk list. No Q3 AI figure verified this run, and the C$700m-1bn composite target flagged as yesterday's catalyst was not updated in anything retrieved.
  • National Bank of Canada (Q3 FY26, 27 Aug) - net income $1,307m, +23%. No AI attribution found.
  • PYMNTS retail roundup (20 Aug) re-read for names - every figure in it (Walmart, Amazon, Target, Albertsons, Shopify, Etsy) was already logged from the primary calls.
The numerator-without-denominator pattern is now six companies deep and hardening into a disclosure convention. Walmart, Amazon, Albertsons, Target, Shopify and now Williams-Sonoma have each published a ratio - AOV differential, conversion multiple, growth rate - and none has published the base it sits on. Etsy remains the only retailer to disclose the denominator, and it was under 1%. Williams-Sonoma's 9x is the most extreme ratio in the log and also the most self-selected.

  • S3 insurance, new risk classes - Sixfold AI Underwriter (hit ratios +15%, GWP per underwriter +30%), BriteCore copilots, Armilla AI liability cover - all Tier D vendor claims with no deploying carrier named and no bound premium. Fails the inclusion bar and the Tier D rule together.

Every source was a billing-vendor explainer with no named provider and no collected revenue. Held as a structural catalyst, not an entry.
  • S3 industrials and telecom, new SKUs - SK Telecom AIDC revenue +92.5% to KRW 136.2bn, Fujikura ordinary profit up 2.7x on AI data-centre demand. Both are AI-as-customer: pre-existing capacity and fibre sold to a new buyer. Out of frame by definition.

  • S2 hospitality, AI revenue management - hotel-tech trade press quoting ADR +10-15% and RevPAR +8-15%. No named operator, no earnings source, vendor-authored.

Williams-Sonoma, Inc. Retail · US S1 — Velocity

On the Q2 FY2026 call (26 Aug 2026) Chief Technology and Digital Officer Sameer Hassan said of the Williams Sonoma brand's AI shopping assistant: "Engagement with Olive is up 700%. Revenue is up 620%, and customers who engage with Olive convert at 3 times a higher rate", and that "A visit where we personalize the experience now generates roughly 9 times the revenue of an average visit. Last year, that was 2X." A second rendering of the same transcript dates both Olive figures "since the beginning of the year". Total Q2 revenue was $1.96bn, +6.7%, with comparable brand revenue +6.2%; none of it was attributed to AI and no incremental dollars were sized. CEO Laura Alber's only quantified framing was qualitative - "AI is an accelerator to our strategy and our productivity."

Mechanism
Olive is a conversational shopping assistant on the Williams Sonoma brand site that takes open-ended intents and returns product sets; the disclosed revenue path is conversion, with engagers converting at 3x the site rate. Separately, an e-commerce personalization layer selects on-site content per visit, and Williams-Sonoma measures a personalized visit against an average visit. The 9x figure is a ratio between two visit populations, not a lift measured against a held-out control.
Why this shape
S1, not S2, because the load-bearing numbers are input metrics - engagement, a conversion multiple, and a revenue-per-visit ratio. "Revenue is up 620%" is growth in revenue transacted through the Olive surface off an undisclosed base, which is the denominator game METHOD names: a numerator with no base is not attributed incremental revenue and cannot promote to S2. Revenue conversion was conspicuously absent: no dollars, no share of e-commerce, no statement that any of the 6.2% comp was Olive-driven. The 9x personalized-visit figure is confounded by self-selection - high-intent visitors are more likely both to be personalized to and to buy - and Williams-Sonoma offered no counterfactual.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source

Q2 FY2026 earnings call transcript, 2026-08-26
Falsifier
Williams-Sonoma puts a base under Olive in a later quarter - dollars or a share of digital revenue - and it lands immaterial, under roughly 1-2% of e-commerce, as Etsy's under-1% traffic disclosure did for agentic channels. Equally falsifying: the 700%/620%/3x growth rates vanish from disclosure once the base is large enough to be embarrassing, or comps decelerate while the Olive ratios keep compounding.

Williams-Sonoma, Inc. Retail · US C4 — Non-tech · non-engineering

On the same Q2 FY2026 call (26 Aug 2026) Sameer Hassan said of Otto, the assistant launched for the Pottery Barn brands: "Over 70% of engagements with Otto are able to be resolved without handing you over to a person." No dollar saving, no headcount action, no service-cost-per-contact figure was attached, and Williams-Sonoma did not say what the pre-Otto deflection rate was.

Mechanism
A customer-service assistant fronting the Pottery Barn brands' contact channels, resolving order, product and post-purchase queries without escalation to a human agent. The cost path is contact deflection - each resolved engagement is an agent-handled contact not incurred - but the company disclosed the deflection rate, not the contact cost or the volume it applies to.
Why this shape
C4 - non-tech company, non-engineering function. Logged on the same footing as Woolworths Team Assist (26 Aug 2026): a specific, quantified deflection rate from a Tier A source with no dollar attached. METHOD trap 5 applies in advance: even at face value this is cost avoidance on contacts, not a reduction in a disclosed expense line, and without a baseline deflection rate the 70% cannot be read as incremental. Logged separately from the Olive S1 entry because it is a distinct disclosure about a distinct product, not a second reading of the same number.

Williams-Sonoma never converts the deflection rate into a service-cost figure - cost per contact, customer-service expense down, or SG&A leverage attributed to Otto - in FY2026 or FY2027 disclosure, or the 70% figure disappears from later calls without a successor metric.

Two entries, both from one company - Woolworths' FY26 results - and no S3 anywhere. The trailing 48 hours were a bank-and-retail tape, and the pattern is that large financials narrate AI hard and quantify it not at all.

  • Bath & Body Works and Abercrombie & Fitch (Q2, 26 Aug) - results released, no AI-attributed figure in the releases retrieved.

Both are grocers with a conversational assistant; Albertsons put 10% and 26% average-order-value numbers on it in August, Woolworths put a user count on it. The metric plainly exists and is disclosable, which makes the silence a choice rather than an absence.

Woolworths Group Grocery / Retail · Australia S1 — Velocity

On the FY2026 results call (25-26 Aug 2026) CEO Amanda Bardwell said that "leveraging agentic AI, Olive, our much-loved digital shopping companion, has transformed into a personal shopping companion", with the results presentation disclosing "over 1 million unique customers who used the tool". Group eCommerce sales rose 15.9% to $10.6bn and average weekly digital visits reached 14 million, up 22.9%; neither was attributed to AI. No AI-attributed revenue, basket-size or conversion figure was given, and Bardwell said of Olive that "it is still early days".

Mechanism
Olive is a conversational assistant in the Woolworths app that Bardwell says customers use for "recipe inspiration, meal planning, and product discovery". The stated revenue path is basket construction: Smart Basket, launched in the period, "helps customers build their online baskets faster through predictive additions based on purchasing habits", which Bardwell said is "seeing positive engagement and customer retention rates" - no engagement or retention number was attached to either claim. The presentation also states "connected customers who engaged with digital platforms spent 2.4 times more than in-store-only customers in Q4", but that is a channel statistic Woolworths did not attribute to AI and is straightforwardly confounded by who shops online.
Why this shape
S1, and at the weak end of it. The only AI-specific number is adoption - unique users - not throughput, conversion or hit rate, so this sits a step below the Walmart and Albertsons disclosures already in the log, which at least offered a cohort spend differential. Revenue conversion is conspicuously absent rather than merely unstated: Woolworths sized eCommerce growth (15.9%), digital visits (+22.9%), app share of orders (>70% in Q4) and a 2.4x connected-customer spend gap on the same day, and attributed none of them to Olive, Smart Basket or any AI capability. Not S2 - nothing incremental is attributed. Not S3 - grocery baskets are the definition of pre-existing demand.

Woolworths Group Grocery / Retail · Australia C4 — Non-tech · non-engineering

In the same FY2026 results (26 Aug 2026) Bardwell said the Team Assist platform is "already managing over 7,000 queries per week and resolving nine in 10 before reaching our advisory team", and the presentation states "AI-powered catalogue planning reduced production time from a week to a few hours". No dollar saving, no headcount effect and no inference or licensing run-cost was disclosed against either.

Mechanism
Two separate non-engineering deployments at a retailer with roughly 200,000 employees. Team Assist is a retrieval-and-answer agent sitting in front of an internal advisory team, deflecting queries that would otherwise consume adviser time. The marketing assistant suite applies AI "across planning, drafting, and production" of the weekly catalogue, compressing a weekly production cycle into hours.
Why this shape
C4 - non-tech company, non-engineering function, the cell METHOD.md weights most heavily. Two standing traps apply and neither is resolved by the disclosure. Trap 1, adoption dressed as saving: 7,000 queries and a 90% deflection rate measure volume handled, not an expense line that fell. Trap 5, cost avoidance versus realized reduction: a catalogue produced in hours instead of a week frees marketing capacity, and Woolworths did not say whether that capacity was banked or reinvested. Group cost of doing business was discussed on the call but never attributed to AI. Logged in the cost ledger only - the Olive/Smart Basket revenue-side disclosure is logged separately as S1 and these are distinct claims, not one claim double-counted. Source discrepancy worth flagging: the call transcript describes Team Assist as resolving queries "before reaching our advisory team", i.e. team-facing, while the slides rendering says "resolved 9 in 10 customer interactions without human intervention". The CEO's verbatim wording is taken as authoritative; the slides rendering may be an aggregator paraphrase.

  • Sysco - Q4 FY2026 release (4 Aug, 8-K exhibit), reconfirmed 20 Aug: $100m of FY2027 efficiency improvements "driven by an artificial intelligence (AI)-powered transformation". Cost only, no revenue target anywhere in either release. Logged C4, PROJECTED.
  • Deere & Company - Q3 FY2026 call (20 Aug): 520m engaged acres, 1.2m connected machines, See & Spray on about one-third of North American sprayers on order. Not logged - see near-misses.
  • Walmart, Target, Albertsons, Amazon - re-read through a 20 Aug PYMNTS roundup. Every figure in it was already in this log from the primary calls; no new disclosure, and the roundup added no denominator to any of them.
The pattern across runs is now sharp: the retail agentic-commerce cluster is all numerator and no denominator. Four companies have disclosed growth rates and per-order lifts; Etsy is the first to disclose the base, and the base is under 1% of traffic. Both things are true at once - the channel is real and it is immaterial.

Near-misses deliberately not logged

  • S3 industrials, autonomy as a new line - Deere & Company Q3 FY2026 - failed the capability gate and the result bar together. Deanna Kovar said Deere "will soon build on that foundation with AI enabled capabilities", future tense with no result, and the engaged-acre and See & Spray figures are precision-ag adoption metrics whose underlying technology long predates the frontier-model era.
  • S3 insurance, newly-serveable risk - carrier product launches, GWP disclosures and AI-liability coverage - every hit was a vendor platform (Sixfold, Cytora, DeNexus) or an insurtech (Corgi), Tier D and out of scope as subject. No incumbent carrier disclosed premium bound on a class it previously declined.
  • S3 pharma, unscreenable target space - no AI-discovered drug has an approval, so there is no revenue to disclose. Every hit was a market-size forecast, which fails the named-company and specific-claim tests.
  • S2 QSR, drive-thru upsell - Wendy's FreshAI "15% increase in upsell attachment" traces to a 2025 investor day. Outside any defensible window and not retrieved from a primary source this run.
  • S2 retail, agentic conversion - a claim that Walmart's ChatGPT Instant Checkout converts "three times lower" appeared only in a search-engine synthesis. The Forrester post it was attributed to (28 May 2026) contains neither Walmart, nor Instant Checkout, nor any conversion figure. Dropped as aggregator misattribution.
  • Counter-evidence, the "95% of AI pilots fail" study - content-farm renderings now describe its design as "2,400 enterprises across 15 industries over 18 months", which is not the MIT NANDA study. Reconstructed numbers, not logged.
  • AI-as-customer, not AI ROI - Caterpillar, Applied Industrial Technologies, Bloom Energy and Duke Energy all showed AI-build-out demand. Pre-existing SKUs sold to a new buyer; out of frame by definition, not by a close judgement call.
  • S1 medtech - GE HealthCare and Philips Q2 2026 both described their portfolios as AI-enabled. Neither broke out an AI SKU number, so there is nothing to test.

Nearest dated catalysts

Etsy Retail / E-commerce marketplace · US S1 — Velocity⚠ Counter-evidence

On the Q2 2026 call (6 Aug 2026) CEO Kruti Patel Goyal said: "Traffic from agentic experiences is still less than 1% of our overall traffic. Logged 18 days after the call because it is the only hard ceiling any retailer has put on agentic traffic.

Mechanism
Third-party AI assistants refer shoppers into Etsy listings; Etsy's own Gifting Assistant beta offers conversational gift discovery on-platform. Both are top-of-funnel referral and discovery against the existing catalogue - no new product, no new seller supply, no new price point.
Why this shape
S1, and counter-evidence to it. The disclosed number is an input metric - share of total traffic - not revenue, which is the S1 test. Revenue conversion was conspicuously absent: management characterised the traffic as higher-intent and higher-AOV but attached no GMS or revenue figure, and said it was seeing "the same things that we shared last time", so this is a second quarter of qualitative-only characterisation. Logged as counter-evidence because it is the first named-company denominator for a channel the log's retail cluster (Walmart Sparky, Target, Lowe's Mylow, Albertsons Ask AI, Amazon Alexa for Shopping) has described only in growth rates and per-order lifts. Nothing here is untrue; the rounding simply runs one way.

Aviva plc Insurance · UK S2 — Share

On the H1 2026 results call (14 Aug 2026) CEO Amanda Blanc said: "U.K. Personal Lines is a great example. We have used AI in our pricing models to deliver over GBP 200 million of run rate benefits here. That is on top of GBP 100 million of claims cost savings previously mentioned." The GBP 200 million figure appears nowhere in the H1 2026 investor presentation, which quantifies AI only as accuracy and time-reduction metrics plus approximately GBP 40 million of claims cost reductions against a stated ambition of more than GBP 50 million.

Mechanism
Machine-learned pricing models in UK personal motor and home: risk selection and demand-elastic rate-setting at the quote, so Aviva wins the risks it prices better than the market and declines or loads the ones it does not. Aviva has said it has used AI and machine learning in pricing "for over a decade," so this is an accumulated run-rate, not a frontier-model effect.
Why this shape
S2, not S3. Test: absent the AI, would this buyer have bought similar cover from somebody? In the UK, motor insurance is compulsory and home insurance near-universal among mortgaged owners - every one of these policies would have been written by some carrier. Better pricing moves the policy from a competitor to Aviva and moves margin within the industry; it creates no new demand and no new insurable population. Zero-sum at industry level. Standing trap 3 applies with force: "run rate benefits" is a company-defined composite that Aviva does not decompose, and it is not a revenue number. The one thing that argues for the revenue ledger over the cost ledger is that Blanc explicitly separates it from the claims cost savings in the same sentence, which places it on the pricing and premium side of the P&L rather than the expense side. If a later disclosure shows the GBP 200 million is predominantly loss-ratio improvement, this entry belongs in C4 and should be moved.

Aviva plc Insurance · UK S1 — Velocity

On the same H1 2026 call Blanc said AI has cut commercial-lines quote generation "from days to hours, which is driving higher conversion," and that in Protection "we have halved the amount of time it takes to review each case in medical underwriting with near perfect accuracy." The written presentation states the underwriting figure as "more than 45% reduction in case summarization time" at "99.99% accuracy." No bound premium, conversion rate or new-business figure is attached to either claim.

Mechanism
Two separate revenue-producing processes. In commercial lines, automated submission ingestion and risk digitisation compress the broker quote turnaround, and in a broker-intermediated market the first acceptable quote back wins a disproportionate share. In Protection, an AI case summariser compresses medical underwriting review, shortening the gap between application and policy issue where applications lapse.
Why this shape
S1: both disclosed numbers are input metrics - cycle time and accuracy - not revenue. Revenue conversion is conspicuously absent, and the absence is specific rather than general: Blanc asserts the quote-time compression is "driving higher conversion" and then gives no conversion figure, in the same speech in which she was willing to quantify a GBP 200 million pricing benefit. Aviva's H1 personal-lines premium growth of roughly 98% is the Direct Line acquisition, not AI, and the company does not claim otherwise. Note also the venue discrepancy on the same metric: "halved" spoken, "more than 45%" written, and "review" spoken versus "case summarization" written - the spoken version is both rounder and broader.

InterContinental Hotels Group (IHG) Hospitality · UK S2 — Share

On the H1 2026 results call (11 Aug 2026) CEO Elie Maalouf cited "the rollout of our AI-enhanced revenue management system, which has unlocked revenue uplift and market share gains for our owners." No magnitude was attached, and the H1 results announcement published the same day contains no mention of AI at all. Group RevPAR was +4.1% and operating profit +10% to $665m; neither was attributed to AI.

Mechanism
An AI-enhanced revenue management system setting room rates across franchised and managed hotels. The uplift accrues to hotel owners as RevPAR and index gain; IHG monetises it indirectly through percentage-of-revenue fees. Separately in the half Maalouf said IHG "launched new AI-enabled natural language search capabilities within our award-winning mobile app and our website" and "launched an IHG plug-in within ChatGPT", both without any disclosed booking or conversion metric.
Why this shape
S2 on the CEO's own wording. "Market share gains for our owners" is a RevPAR-index claim: the index is measured against a competitive set, so the gain is taken from the hotel across the road. Absent the system the guest still books a room somewhere, so the counterfactual purchase happens - fails S3 test (a). Fails test (c) as well: yield management by machine learning is a 1990s hospitality technology, not a capability that appeared in the last 24 months. Zero-sum at industry level and GDP-neutral.

Carnegie Mellon University / Larridin - 'Do AI Adoption Signals Predict Company Performance?' (564 companies, 538 after dedup, 478 10-K filings, 30,000+ job postings) Cross-sector (research) · US S2 — Share⚠ Counter-evidence

Cross-sectional study surfaced 17 Aug 2026 testing whether public AI-adoption signals - vendor tracker scores, AI hiring intensity, investment intensity and 10-K disclosure language - predict revenue growth, margin improvement or stock returns across 538 companies in 12 sectors. Only one signal survived: narrative concreteness, where firms at the top of the distribution are associated with 8.0 percentage points higher year-over-year revenue growth than those at the bottom (coefficient +0.080, p=0.009). Broader adoption measures weakened under multiple-testing correction, adoption showed little connection to margins, and no public AI signal predicted four-month forward stock returns. AI infrastructure suppliers beat sector- and size-matched peers by roughly 32 points over four months.

Mechanism
No deployment mechanism - this is an attempt to price the deployment mechanism from the outside, using signals an investor can actually observe. That is what makes the null result load-bearing: if AI adoption were producing revenue at scale, the observable proxies for adoption should carry some of it.
Why this shape
Filed against S2 because that is the shape it tests and fails to confirm - attributed incremental revenue on existing products at adopting firms. Counter-evidence, with one important qualification: the surviving coefficient is on how specifically a company *describes* its AI, not on how much AI it has. That is at least as consistent with reverse causality - firms already growing have concrete things to describe - as with AI causing growth, and the paper's own signals cannot separate the two. The infrastructure-supplier result is the picks-and-shovels finding restated: the measurable return sits with the sellers, not the deployers.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier C — Third-party reporting — journalism, trade press, sell-side
CMU-Larridin working paper (PDF retrieved this run); interpretation via Help Net Security, 17 Aug 2026, 2026-08-17
Falsifier
A later panel with the same construction in which adoption intensity, not disclosure concreteness, predicts forward revenue growth - or the concreteness coefficient surviving a design with firm fixed effects and lagged growth controls. Either would flip this from artifact to signal.
Confidence
low — Working paper, not peer-reviewed, and co-authored with Larridin - a vendor that sells the AI-adoption scores the paper evaluates, and whose commercial interest points at the one positive finding. The +0.080 / p=0.009 coefficient was verified in the retrieved PDF; the PDF's text layer was not machine-extractable this run, so the surrounding prose rests on the Help Net Security rendering.

2026-08-21

permalink →

Assessment

Four findings, all S1, and they are the same finding wearing five company logos. No S3 again - the standing count is one S3-CANDIDATE in the whole log. What the week produced instead is a disclosure convention: the AI cohort differential, a metric that cannot establish incrementality and is now the house style at four of the largest retailers in the US.

Sources read

  • Walmart - Q2 FY2027 call, 20 Aug. Furner: Sparky users +70% y/y and spending 40% more per order. Up from 35% on the Q4 FY26 call in February, so the claim held and rose. eCommerce +23%, advertising +38%, neither attributed to AI, no incremental dollars. Logged S1.
  • Lowe's - Q2 2026 call, 19 Aug. Mylow users convert at 3x non-users; ~25m questions fielded since launch. No ticket, basket or dollar figure. Logged S1.
  • Albertsons - WSJ interview, 17 Aug. SVP Jill Pavlovich: AOV +10% on conversational search, +26% on the fuller assistants. This is the figure that disappeared from the 23 Jul earnings call and has now reappeared in a newspaper. Logged S1, and the venue is the point.
  • Boozt - Q2 2026 call, 14 Aug. The one retailer that published the base rate: 3% of customers use the AI assistant, and those who do convert at 2.5x and spend 8% more per order. Group revenue +13%, EBIT margin nearly doubled, neither attributed to AI. Also 10,000 synthetic model images since April at ~300 variants a day from 16 reusable models. Logged S1, and the 3% is why.
  • Home Depot - Q2'26 call, 18 Aug. Magic Apron at "millions of questions per month", Pros who use the AI Material List Builder "purchase more". Not one number attached to any of it. Same week, same category, same mechanism as Lowe's - and Home Depot declined to produce the cohort statistic. Not logged: fails inclusion bar #2.
  • Deere - Q3 FY26 call, 20 Aug. The scheduled S3 test on the board, and it came back negative. 520m engaged acres, >190m highly engaged, ~450k monthly digital users, See & Spray heading to ~1/3 of North American sprayers on order at >50% herbicide savings. Zero revenue dollars against precision or autonomy, and no separated subscription line. Third consecutive examination of Deere's precision franchise with adoption metrics and no money.
  • Ross Stores (20 Aug) - one AI answer in Q&A, "AI is everywhere... in every conference room", no metric. BJ's Wholesale (21 Aug) - no AI mention in the release. Chunghwa Telecom (5 Aug) - AI revenue is IDC and AI-supply-chain projects, i.e. selling into the buildout, out of frame by the Verizon precedent.
  • CGTrader / Fortune (20 Aug) - counter-evidence, appendix. AI assets are 1 in 6 new uploads and ~1.1% of revenue.
The pattern, stated plainly: the industry has standardised on a metric that cannot be wrong. Walmart says Sparky users spend 40% more per order. Amazon said Alexa for Shopping users spend "over 40% more per order" three weeks earlier. Albertsons says 10% and 26% more per basket. Lowe's says 3x conversion. Every one is a comparison between people who chose to use an assistant and people who did not - and the shoppers most likely to open an AI assistant are the ones already deepest in intent. Not one of the four disclosed an incremental dollar, a control group, or a base rate. Amazon is the tell: two quarters ago it put a ~$12bn incremental annualised sales figure on its shopping assistant, and has since replaced it with the cohort statistic. And Boozt is the control: the one company that did publish its base rate published 3%, which makes a 2.5x conversion multiple worth a rounding error on a 13% revenue line. There is no reason to assume the four US retailers who withheld the denominator are sitting on a better one.

Near-misses deliberately not logged

  • Deere precision ag - adoption metrics only, no revenue dollars. Fails inclusion bar #2 on the revenue line, same as the Precision Essentials rejection on 2026-07-27. The >50% herbicide saving is a customer's cost, at unnamed customers, and belongs to neither ledger.
  • Home Depot Magic Apron - named company, named mechanism, real scale, no number. Bar #2.
  • NBER 34984 / Atlanta Fed (Baslandze et al., ~750 executives, March 2026) - reports a "productivity paradox" with perceived gains exceeding measured gains "likely reflecting a delay in revenue realizations". Directionally the most interesting sentence retrieved today, but it is a five-month-old executive survey with no named company and no revenue figure, and three comparable surveys are already logged. Padding, not evidence.
  • GM autonomy subscriptions ($3.1bn 2026 revenue, $7.5bn deferred) - a projection, from a Tier C secondary in June, and GM does not call it AI. Fails the retrieval and attribution tests both.
  • Chunghwa Telecom, SK Telecom - AI-linked revenue growth that is infrastructure sold to the buildout. Shovels, not results.
  • Aviva H1 2026, specialty insurance sweep, pharma/biotech sweep, thin-file lending sweep, professional-services sweep - no named incumbent put a number on the revenue line. The insurance S3 hunting ground has now been dry for four consecutive weeks; every result returns vendor marketing.

Nearest dated catalysts

  • Albertsons Q2 FY2026 call, ~Oct 2026 - the sharpest test on the board: does the 10%/26% figure survive contact with an SEC-exposed venue, or does it live only in press interviews?
  • Walmart Q3 FY2027, ~Nov - does the 40% per-order gap widen, hold, or compress as Sparky penetration rises? Compression is the selection-bias signature.
  • Boozt Q3 2026, late Oct - whether AI assistant adoption clears 3%; the cleanest published test of whether the cohort gap survives dilution.
  • Lowe's Q3 2026, ~Nov - whether 3x is repeated and whether it is ever converted into ticket or digital-sales dollars.
  • Medtronic Q1 FY2027, 1 Sep - next healthcare device print with AI-enabled SKUs; not 18 Aug as some aggregators listed.
  • Roche Pharma Day, 28 Sep - the standing pharma S3 checkpoint set on 2026-07-26.
Walmart Retail · US S1 — Velocity

On the Q2 FY2027 call (20 Aug 2026) CEO John Furner said: "The number of customers using Sparky is up 70% from last year, and the customers and members who use Sparky for shopping spend 40% more per order than others who do not." The per-order differential is up from the 35% Walmart disclosed on the Q4 FY2026 call (19 Feb 2026). Global eCommerce grew 23% and global advertising 38% in the quarter; neither was attributed to AI and no incremental sales dollars were sized.

Mechanism
Sparky, a conversational assistant inside the Walmart app, answers open-ended shopping intents (Furner's example: a weekly high-protein meal plan) and returns recipes, meal kits and a one-click basket of ingredients, plus recognition of items the shopper already bought. The revenue path is cross-category basket building - the assistant converts a single-item errand into a multi-category order.
Why this shape
S1, not S2, and the distinction follows the Amazon precedent logged 2026-08-12: a cohort spend differential is an input/behavioural metric, not attributed incremental revenue. Nobody has established that the same shopper spends more because of Sparky; the disclosed statistic is equally consistent with high-frequency, high-basket shoppers being the ones who try an assistant first. Revenue conversion is conspicuously absent rather than merely unstated - Walmart sized eCommerce (+23%) and advertising (+38%) growth on the same call and attributed neither to AI. Note the convergence: Walmart's cohort statistic (40% more per order) is now numerically identical to Amazon's Alexa for Shopping figure from three weeks earlier. Two of the largest retailers on earth have settled on the same non-causal disclosure format.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier A — Primary and accountable — filing, transcript, formal guidance
Q2 FY2027 earnings call (transcript via Investing.com, cross-checked against the TradingKey transcript and PYMNTS coverage); prior 35% figure via Modern Retail coverage of the Q4 FY2026 call, 2026-08-20
Falsifier
If the differential is causal it should persist or widen as Sparky moves from early adopters to the median shopper; if it is selection, it must compress as penetration rises. Usage is already +70% y/y, so Q3/Q4 FY27 is the test. Treat as narrative if Walmart drops the per-order figure, replaces it with adoption-only metrics (users, questions answered), or never converts it into an incremental-sales dollar figure by the FY27 close.
Confidence
low — Tier A, named CEO, verbatim quote verified across two transcripts - high confidence it was said. Low confidence as evidence of incremental revenue: an uncontrolled cohort comparison with unaddressed selection bias and no dollars attached.
Lowe's Companies Retail · US S1 — Velocity

On the Q2 2026 call (19 Aug 2026) Lowe's disclosed that "customers who engage with Mylow while browsing online convert at three times the rate of those who don't", and that Mylow has fielded approximately 25 million customer and associate questions since launch. No revenue, basket or ticket figure was attributed to Mylow, and the quarter's positive comparable sales were not attributed to AI.

Mechanism
Mylow is a generative assistant on lowes.com answering home-improvement project questions in session; the same engine powers a Mylow Companion application for store associates. The claimed revenue path is intent resolution - a shopper who gets a project question answered mid-session completes the purchase instead of abandoning.
Why this shape
S1: conversion rate is an input metric, not revenue. Revenue conversion conspicuously absent - Lowe's put no dollars, ticket or basket figure against Mylow on the same call where it reported comps and raised its store target. Selection bias is severe and unaddressed in the specific direction that inflates the number: a shopper who stops to ask a project question is deeper in purchase intent than the average browser, so a 3x conversion gap is the expected result even if Mylow were inert. Not S2 because no incremental revenue is attributed; not S3 because these buyers were already buying home-improvement product from Lowe's or a competitor.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier A — Primary and accountable — filing, transcript, formal guidance
Q2 2026 earnings call (full transcript via Benzinga; figure cross-checked against Retail TouchPoints coverage of the same call), 2026-08-19
Falsifier
A 3x gap should compress toward 1x as Mylow reaches the median shopper rather than the self-selected early cohort. Treat as narrative if Lowe's stops repeating the multiple, or if by FY27 it has still never converted it into a ticket, basket or digital-sales dollar figure. Direct comparator on the board: Home Depot ran the same play in the same week (Magic Apron, millions of questions per month) and disclosed no conversion figure at all.
Confidence
low — Tier A call and the figure is confirmed in two independent renderings of the transcript. Low as ROI evidence: an uncontrolled cohort ratio with no baseline conversion rate, no denominator and no dollars.
Albertsons Companies Grocery · US S1 — Velocity

In a Wall Street Journal interview published 17 Aug 2026, Albertsons SVP of Digital Shopping Experiences Jill Pavlovich said average order value rises 10% when customers use the company's conversational search and 26% when they use its more comprehensive assistants that match recipes and ingredients to dietary preferences. Follow-up on the entry logged 2026-07-26: the 10% figure is the same one CEO Susan Morris put on the record on 7 Jan 2026 and did NOT repeat on the 23 Jul Q1 FY2026 call. It has reappeared - in a press interview rather than an SEC-exposed venue - accompanied by a higher second figure.

Mechanism
Conversational search plus a fuller meal-planning assistant that resolves a dietary constraint into a recipe and then into an ingredient list. Pavlovich's stated mechanism is category breadth: "All of a sudden they're shopping across categories, across items, not spearfishing for that single item that they need".
Why this shape
S1 by the same test applied to Amazon (2026-08-12) and Walmart today: an average-order-value differential between users and non-users is a cohort/behavioural input metric, not attributed incremental revenue. The 26% figure is the more interesting one and the weaker one - it describes the deepest-engagement cohort, which is also the most self-selected. What makes this entry worth logging is not the number but the venue. The claim vanished from the July earnings call and returned in a newspaper interview eight weeks later. Partial reverse-falsification of the 2026-07-26 entry, whose falsifier asked whether Albertsons would re-disclose a revenue-attributed AI metric at or above 10% - it has, but not where securities liability attaches.

The clean test is the Q2 FY2026 call (expected Oct 2026): does Morris put the 10% or 26% figure on an earnings call, or does it stay in press interviews? If AI revenue metrics only ever appear in trade and newspaper venues and never in the SEC-exposed one, that asymmetry is itself the finding and this entry should be re-read as evidence of disclosure arbitrage rather than of revenue.

Boozt AB Retail / E-commerce fashion · SE S1 — Velocity

On the Q2 2026 earnings call (14 Aug 2026) CEO Hermann Haraldsson said "only around 3% of our customers use our AI assistant so far", that those users "convert at roughly 2.5 times our normal rate" and "spend about 8% more per order". Separately he said that since launching AI model images in April Boozt has "produced around 10,000 of them", "adding roughly 300 new variants a day from just 16 reusable models", with "no casting cost at all". Group revenue rose 13% and EBIT margin nearly doubled; neither was attributed to AI.

Mechanism
The same in-session assistant mechanism as Walmart's Sparky, Lowe's Mylow and Albertsons' Ask AI - but Boozt is the only retailer in the set to publish the denominator, and the denominator is the finding. At ~3% penetration a 2.5x conversion multiple and an 8% order-value gap cannot move a group revenue line that grew 13%. The AI model imagery is a separate, cost-side mechanism: synthetic on-model product photography replacing photo shoots, which is why no revenue attaches to it.
Why this shape
S1: conversion rate and average order value are input metrics. This entry earns its place next to the four US retail cohort statistics logged today because it is the control on them. Revenue conversion here is not merely absent, it is arithmetically ruled out by the company's own adoption figure - and Haraldsson did not claim otherwise. Fails S3 on test (a): existing customers buying existing fashion inventory from the same retailer. The imagery disclosure is folded into this entry rather than opened as a separate C4 cost entry because Boozt gave a throughput number (10,000 images, ~300/day, 16 reusable models) and a qualitative 'no casting cost', not a cost figure - on its own it does not clear inclusion bar #2.

Falsifier
If the Q3 2026 report (late October) shows AI assistant adoption still around 3%, the 2.5x conversion multiple is a selection artifact of Boozt's most engaged customers rather than a lever. Confirmation would be adoption climbing into double digits with the conversion gap surviving the dilution.

CGTrader Digital marketplace (3D assets) · LT S3 — New TAM⚠ Counter-evidence

Marketplace transaction data covering June 2025 - May 2026, reported by Fortune on 20 Aug 2026: AI-generated models are now roughly 1 in 6 newly uploaded assets on CGTrader but account for "$1 out of every $90 in generated revenue" (~1.1%) and 2.6% of sales. Only 5% of CGTrader customers who tried an AI-generated asset found it worked well, against 20% who found it inadequate. Alongside: Pew reports 52% of US adults are "more concerned than excited" about greater AI use, up from 38% in 2022.

Mechanism
Generative models collapsed the cost of producing a 3D asset, so supply expanded roughly six-fold as a share of new uploads. The S3 hypothesis this tests is the newly-economic one: if AI-collapsed production cost opens demand that previously could not be served, revenue share should track supply share. It does not - a ~17% share of new supply converts to ~1-3% of transactions.
Why this shape
Counter-evidence tagged to S3, and specifically to test (d), newly economic. This is the cleanest natural experiment retrieved to date on whether an AI-driven cost collapse creates demand: the cost curve moved hard, supply followed, and buyers did not. It contradicts the standing assumption that cheaper production is sufficient for new TAM - on this marketplace it produced catalogue inflation, not a new market. Appendix scope because CGTrader is an online software marketplace and out of scope as a subject in the main revenue log; the finding is about buyer behaviour, not about CGTrader's own AI deployment. Two figures are given for the same phenomenon (1.1% of revenue, 2.6% of sales); read them as revenue share and unit share respectively - Fortune does not reconcile them and neither should this entry.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier C — Third-party reporting — journalism, trade press, sell-side
Fortune, reporting CGTrader marketplace data (June 2025 - May 2026) and Pew Research August 2026, 2026-08-20
Falsifier
If AI-asset revenue share on CGTrader rises materially toward its supply share over the next two reporting periods, the discount is a quality-and-trust lag rather than a demand ceiling, and this entry becomes a snapshot of an early market rather than evidence against test (d). Also falsified if the gap is shown to be a ranking or labelling artefact of the marketplace itself rather than buyer preference.
Confidence
medium — Real transaction data over a 12-month window from a named marketplace, which is far better than survey sentiment - but Tier C, single-outlet, self-reported by the marketplace, one narrow asset category, and the two revenue-share figures are not reconciled in the source.

Assessment

Two findings, no S3 - the eighteenth day of nineteen with the priority bucket empty. Target is the first large US retailer in this log to put a specific AI-attributed conversion number on a Tier A call, and it is still an input metric with no dollars behind it. Rakuten attributes a whole segment's 15.6% growth to AI without decomposing any of it.

Sources read

  • Target Q2 2026 call and release (19 Aug). CMO Cara Sylvester: AI-powered Teacher and College Wish List recommendations drove wish-list creations +more than 50%, items added more than doubling, and conversion on key back-to-school pages up nearly 20%. Digital comps +8.7%, total comps +3.8%, traffic +3.6% - none attributed to AI. CEO Michael Fiddelke added that digital traffic from external AI platforms is growing 3.5x the industry rate, and named a first Chief AI Officer.
  • Rakuten Q2 FY2026 release (10 Aug, 9 days old and unlogged). Advertising revenue 65.6bn yen, +15.6% YoY, "driven by AI-powered search and targeted banner advertising". Group revenue 665.5bn yen, non-GAAP operating income 42.0bn yen +109.6%. The release also notes AI development costs are now allocated into each business from Q1/26.
  • Lowe's Q2 2026 release (19 Aug). Comps +0.2%, online sales +15.7%, full-year guidance cut to the bottom of range. Zero mentions of AI, Mylow or personalisation anywhere in the release - notable given Mylow was the centrepiece of the May call.
  • Estee Lauder FY26 Q4 8-K (19 Aug). Organic net sales +5% in Q4, +3% for the year. Zero AI, personalisation or technology attribution in the release.
  • TJX Q2 FY27 (19 Aug): comps +4%, net income $1.5bn, no AI attribution. Analog Devices FQ3 (19 Aug): revenue $4.02bn +40% on data-centre demand - AI-as-customer, and a chipmaker, so doubly out of frame.
The Target and Rakuten pair is the cleanest illustration yet of the two ways revenue-side AI evidence fails. Target discloses a real number that is not revenue. Rakuten discloses real revenue with an attribution that cannot be audited. Neither gives you an incremental AI dollar, and eighteen of nineteen logged days have now produced no S3 at all.

Near-misses deliberately not logged

  • S3 insurance - Munich Re aiSure, Armilla, Corgi, Mayflower Specialty, Embroker standalone AI-liability policies with limits of $2m-$50m. Genuinely new SKUs, but no carrier discloses bound premium, and the risk being priced is somebody else's AI. Fails the specific-number bar.

A real reimbursement pathway, but Aidoc is an AI-native vendor and no named hospital has disclosed revenue from it. Fails the non-tech-subject and named-deployer tests.
  • S3 pharma - 117 AI-enabled assets in human trials across 63 companies per an ASCO 2026 analysis; Insilico registered a Phase III for rentosertib. Pipeline stage and partnership payments, not revenue from a previously unserveable indication. Fails test (a).
  • S3 industrials - Deere reports F3Q26 tomorrow; Zebra's Nucleus and Workcloud AI suites launched in June with no separated subscription revenue. Announcement without a result.
  • S3 professional services - legal AI adoption at 42% of firms, 25% raising fees, Eudia at $105m as an AI-native fixed-fee firm. Survey sentiment plus an AI-native subject. Fails the named-incumbent bar.

  • S3 SMB and telecom/energy - Tellus Energy AI, NTT DATA's Smart AI Agent Ecosystem, utility AI capex of $240bn in 2026. Vendor launches and build-out capex, not a deployed AI revenue line at a non-tech incumbent.

Both are AI-native or platform subjects, so their misses describe vendor economics, not deployment ROI. Routed to neither ledger for the third consecutive run.

Rakuten Group E-commerce / Internet services · Japan S2 — Share

In its Q2 FY2026 results release (10 Aug 2026) Rakuten disclosed that "the advertising business achieved high growth with revenue reaching 65.6 billion yen, up 15.6% YoY, driven by AI-powered search and targeted banner advertising on Rakuten Ichiba and Rakuten Travel". Group revenue was 665.5bn yen (+11.6%) and non-GAAP operating income 42.0bn yen (+109.6%). No AI-specific share of the 15.6% was given.

Mechanism
AI applied to ad search relevance and banner targeting across the Ichiba marketplace and Rakuten Travel, with AI also generating ad creative and running screening and bidding across large campaign volumes. Trade coverage (MediaPost, 10 Aug) adds that Rakuten's conversational chatbot shortened time from item discovery to checkout and raised average order value on Ichiba, with no figure disclosed. The same release notes that "from Q1/26, a portion of AI-related development costs are allocated to each business" - the run-cost offset is being pushed into segment P&L.
Why this shape
S2, and the losers are rival Japanese ad platforms and retail-media networks - these advertisers were already buying inventory somewhere. Fails S3 test (a): the counterfactual purchase clearly exists. Fails (c): search relevance ranking and automated bidding are not a capability that appeared in the last 24 months. The entry's real weakness is undecomposed attribution: the whole 15.6% is narrated as AI-driven with no split against Ichiba GMV growth or ad-load, and no counterfactual offered. Subject scope: Rakuten is an e-commerce, fintech and mobile conglomerate, not a software vendor, hyperscaler or AI-native, so main rather than appendix - the same treatment given to Booking and Expedia in this log.
Status
ESTIMATED — company's own internal attribution
Source
Tier B — Company-issued, unaudited — press release, exec interview
Rakuten Group Q2 FY2026 financial results highlights (company release), 2026-08-10
Falsifier
Advertising revenue growth decelerating back toward Ichiba GMV growth in H2 FY2026 while the AI attribution language persists, or no AI-attributed ad revenue figure ever being separated from segment growth in a later quarter.
Confidence
low — Real dated segment revenue at a named company, but the AI causation is an undecomposed management assertion in a Tier B release with no counterfactual and no AI-specific dollar.

Customers Bancorp Banking · US S1 — Velocity

In its Q2 2026 results release (23 Jul 2026) CEO Sam Sidhu said the bank "also saw positive revenue impact, with select verticals delivering over 100% improvement in prospecting success rates as AI enhanced our ability to identify and pursue the highest-quality opportunities", and disclosed a completed pilot of an AI-powered loan closing process that closed selected commercial loans "in seven days, down from 30 to 60 days typically".

Mechanism
Two distinct revenue-producing processes. (1) Commercial prospecting: AI ranks and targets prospects inside named verticals, raising the share of pursued opportunities that convert to a mandate - a hit-rate effect, not a volume effect. (2) Commercial loan closing: an AI-assisted documentation and closing workflow compresses time-to-close from 30-60 days to 7 in pilot, which frees origination capacity and shortens the interval between commitment and interest-earning asset.
Why this shape
S1, not S2. Both disclosed numbers are input metrics - a prospecting hit rate and a cycle time - and neither is a revenue figure. The revenue conversion is exactly the interesting absence: Sidhu explicitly asserts "positive revenue impact" and then puts no dollar, no basis-point spread and no loan-growth decomposition next to it, in a quarter where loans grew 16.9% and deposits 14.5% for reasons the release attributes to franchise build-out generally. A CEO who is willing to say the words "positive revenue impact" in a Tier A filing and unwilling to size them is the standing pattern of this log, stated unusually plainly.
