Tech subjects are in scope here, unlike the revenue log. A separate ledger: never counted in the S1–S3 revenue totals, because cost evidence is far more abundant than revenue evidence and pooling the two would bury the question this log exists to answer.
BHP Group Mining · Australia C4 — Non-tech · non-engineering

BHP's FY26 results (17 Aug 2026) disclose US$470 million of EBITDA from technology initiatives in FY26 - US$330 million delivered in-year plus a US$140 million exit run-rate - against a target exit run-rate beyond US$650 million in FY27; on the call CEO Brandon Craig said that "by the end of 2026, technology initiatives were delivering incremental EBITDA at an annual run rate of almost $500 million". Autonomous haulage is now roughly 50% of the truck fleet.

Mechanism
Named AI applications sit inside a wider technology programme: AI-led leaching optimisation at Escondida lifting copper recoveries, predictive and preventative maintenance powered by AI to cut unplanned downtime, autonomous haulage across about half the truck fleet, and pit-to-port supply-chain optimisation. The disclosed EBITDA is the programme's output, not any one of these.
Why this shape
C4 - non-tech sector, non-engineering function - and a textbook instance of standing cost trap 3, composite value metrics. The number is attributed to "technology initiatives", not to AI; AI is named as a component and never sized, so no AI-attributed figure can be extracted. It is also not cleanly a cost saving: improved copper recoveries raise saleable output, which is a volume effect, so the composite mixes cost avoidance with production gain. Logged once, in the cost ledger, per the single-ledger rule; the revenue reading is noted here rather than double-logged. The two figures do not obviously reconcile - US$470m FY26 total versus a CEO run rate of "almost $500 million" - which is itself evidence the construct is loosely defined.

Assessment

Three findings, and for the first time in 51 entries the log has something S3-shaped: RadNet's patient-paid AI mammography over-read. It is logged as S3-CANDIDATE, not S3 - it fails the capability-gate test outright - but it is the first entry where "absent the AI, would this buyer have bought anything at all?" has a defensible yes.

Sunday sweep, one day after the last logged run (2026-08-14 was missed, so the window was widened to cover 6-16 Aug). Q2 earnings season is in its tail: the big non-tech retail block reports next week, so this was expected to be thin and mostly was.

Sources read

  • RadNet Q2 2026 call (10 Aug) and results release (9 Aug). CFO Mark Stolper: "Revenue from the EBCD program increased 16%". EBCD is an AI second read on a screening mammogram that the payer does not cover and the patient buys - priced $75 falling to $40 in the DeepHealth study period. Separately, Digital Health CEO Kees Westdorp disclosed $16.1m of AI revenue (+136%), $32.4m segment revenue (+56.5%), and ARR of $105.5m (+97%) of which 63% is now external.
  • CarGurus Q2 2026 call (6 Aug). Guru-driven leads +60% sequentially; PriceVantage bookings +50% sequentially with adopting dealers seeing a median 15% VDP lift and 9% more leads per listing; VINMax promoted listings sold 23% faster with 34% more leads per day. Revenue $251.0m, +13%. No AI-attributed revenue dollars, and full-year guidance unchanged at 10-13%.
  • AIG Q2 2026 call (13 Aug). Underwriters "reviewing more submissions and generating quotes significantly faster" via Underwriting by AIG Assist - and not one number attached to any of it.
  • DeepHealth / RSNA 2024 real-world analysis, 747,604 women across 10 practices: 23% enrolment rising to 33% in the final month, with the paper stating flatly that "AI is not reimbursed". Used to establish the counterfactual the 2026 call did not.
The pattern that survives another run: input metrics are disclosed freely, revenue dollars are not. CarGurus quantifies leads to four decimal places and will not put a dollar next to them; AIG will not even quantify the input. Sixteen day-records in, the log still has zero clean S3 and zero non-tech company willing to state AI-attributed revenue in currency.

The RadNet case is interesting precisely because it inverts the usual failure. The normal S3 pretender has frontier technology and no new buyer. RadNet has a genuinely new buyer - a patient handing over $40 for something no one previously sold - built on unglamorous 2021-era CNN imaging AI. New demand did not require a frontier capability jump. If that generalises, the S3 hunting grounds in METHOD are looking in the wrong place: the gate is willingness to pay at a new price point, not model capability.

  • AIG - fails inclusion bar #2. Qualitative productivity language with no number anywhere in the transcript. Its data-centre "bespoke risk solutions" for AI hyperscalers is separately an AI-as-customer case, not ROI on AI deployment.

Could not be logged: the article returned HTTP 401, and the only fetchable substitute (CSO Online, 16 Apr) traces its named carriers - AIG, Great American, W.R. Berkley - to a Financial Times report from November 2025. Too old and too secondary. Flagged for a later run if a primary filings count surfaces.

RadNet Healthcare · US S3 candidate (unproven)

On the Q2 2026 call (10 Aug 2026) CFO Mark Stolper said "Revenue from the EBCD program increased 16%" - RadNet's Enhanced Breast Cancer Detection, an AI second read on screening mammograms that patients buy out of pocket because payers do not reimburse it. No absolute dollar figure, adoption rate or counterfactual was given on the call.

Mechanism
A screening mammogram is covered by insurance; the AI over-read is not. RadNet offers the patient an opt-in upcharge (priced at $75 falling to $40 during the DeepHealth study period) for Saige-DX to read the study alongside the radiologist, with a second AI pass and escalation to a second radiologist on suspicious findings. The revenue is patient cash on top of a reimbursed exam - AI inference cost is near zero per study, so the entire upcharge is incremental gross margin on a scan the company was already performing.
Why this shape
(a) Counterfactual purchase - PASS. The buyer is the patient, the purchase is optional, and absent the AI there is nothing to buy: the base exam is already paid for by the payer. (b) Prior alternative - PASS. Nobody sold a consumer an AI over-read before this; the nearest analogue is a radiologist double-read, which is not a purchasable US consumer SKU. The DeepHealth RSNA 2024 analysis of 747,604 women across 10 practices recorded 23% enrolment rising to 33% in the final month and states plainly that "AI is not reimbursed" - the closest published evidence that this is net-new self-pay demand. (c) Capability gate - FAIL, and this is the honest weak point. Saige-DX is CNN-era FDA-cleared mammography AI, not a post-2024 frontier capability; 2021 technology would have worked. (d) Newly economic - PASS. A human second read cannot be delivered at $40; near-zero marginal inference cost is what opens the price point. Held at S3-CANDIDATE rather than promoted: the Q2 2026 disclosure is a growth rate with no denominator, management never framed it as incremental demand, and (c) fails outright. It is nonetheless the first entry in this log where the counterfactual purchase question has a defensible yes.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier A — Primary and accountable — filing, transcript, formal guidance
Q2 2026 earnings call (transcript), 2026-08-10
Falsifier
RadNet stops breaking out EBCD growth in later quarters, or discloses an absolute EBCD revenue figure so small it is immaterial to a $622.7m quarter. Ironically the other falsifier is success: if payers begin reimbursing EBCD, the self-pay counterfactual collapses and the revenue becomes ordinary reimbursed imaging - S2, not S3.
Confidence
medium — The 16% is a verified Tier A CFO statement, but it was delivered inside a list of Digital Health product-line growth rates (prostate/neuro +13%, TechLive +38%, enterprise imaging workflow +17.3%), so it is not certain whether the EBCD line being grown is patient self-pay revenue at RadNet's own centres or licensed EBCD sold to third-party providers. That ambiguity is load-bearing for the shape and is not resolved by the transcript.
CarGurus Media / Marketplaces (autos) · US S1 — Velocity

On the Q2 2026 call (6 Aug 2026) CEO Jason Trevisan said "Guru-driven leads up 60% sequentially in the U.S." and that VINMax "promoted listings have sold 23% faster and received 34% more leads per day than comparable non-promoted listings"; President & COO Sam Zales said "PriceVantage bookings grew more than 50% sequentially" and that adopting dealers "saw a median 15% lift in VDPs and 9% lift in leads per listing after adoption". Total revenue was $251.0m, up 13%.

Mechanism
Two distinct paths. Consumer side: Guru is a natural-language shopping layer that lets buyers describe a use case rather than filter a taxonomy, converting browsing into dealer leads. Dealer side: PriceVantage uses demand signal to tell a dealer where market demand for specific vehicles sits, and VINMax decides which listings to promote. Dealers pay a subscription, so the revenue path runs lead volume -> perceived value -> higher average revenue per subscribing dealer (QARSD), not through a transaction take rate.
Why this shape
Every disclosed number is an input metric - leads, vehicle detail page views, days-to-sale, bookings - so S1 by test. Revenue conversion was disclosed only obliquely and never in dollars: the Q2 2026 earnings presentation states new product adoption was the largest driver of sequential QARSD increases for a third consecutive quarter, and total revenue grew 13%, but no AI-attributed revenue figure was given and full-year growth guidance was left unchanged at 10-13%. Treated as main log on the precedent already set by Cars.com and Zillow in this log; the marketplace-vs-tech-platform boundary is genuinely arguable here. Note the 60% and 50% figures are sequential, not year-over-year - a favourable framing that flatters small bases.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier A — Primary and accountable — filing, transcript, formal guidance
Q2 2026 earnings call (transcript), 2026-08-06
Falsifier
QARSD growth decelerates in Q3/Q4 2026 while Guru and PriceVantage adoption keeps climbing - lead volume rising without dealer revenue per dealer following means the AI produced traffic, not willingness to pay. Also watch for the sequential framing quietly disappearing once year-over-year comparisons become available.
Confidence
medium — Figures verified verbatim in a fetched Tier A transcript, but they are vendor-side engagement metrics on the company's own products with no dollar attribution and a sequential denominator.
Appendix — revenue findings of any shape whose subject is out of scope (software / AI-native). Kept visible so the S3 picture is not artificially empty and the incumbent-vs-entrant split stays measurable. Not counted in the revenue totals above.
RadNet (DeepHealth / Digital Health segment) Healthcare · US S2 — Share

On the Q2 2026 call (10 Aug 2026) Digital Health CEO Kees Westdorp disclosed "$16.1 million of AI revenue, up 136% year-over-year", Digital Health revenue of "$32.4 million, up 56.5%", ARR of "$105.5 million, up 97%", and that "External ARR, revenue generated outside of RadNet, now makes up 63% of our ARR base", guiding full-year ARR to over $140m.

An imaging provider productised the AI it built for its own reading workflow and now licenses it to competing hospitals and health systems - Diagnostic Suite, Reporting Pro, AI Studio and clinical AI models - signing approximately $21m of total contract value in the quarter. Why this shape
Routed to the appendix because at this segment RadNet is a software vendor, not a deploying corporation: it is selling the shovels, and METHOD excludes shovel-sellers as subjects of the revenue log even when the parent sits in an in-scope sector. Indosat Ooredoo Hutchison Telecom · Indonesia S2 — Share

In its H1 2026 results release (29 Jul 2026) Indosat reported revenue of IDR30.7 trillion, up 13.1% YoY, and attributed AI-powered customer experiences directly to a revenue-per-user outcome: "These differentiated experiences helped drive a 19.9% YoY increase in data traffic and a 17.3% YoY increase in Average Revenue Per User (ARPU)", to IDR46 thousand. The named experiences are the Anti-Spam and Anti-Scam feature, Sahabat-AI, Gemini AI and Adobe Express. Mobile subscribers were flat at 93.4 million, so the ARPU gain is monetisation of an existing base rather than customer acquisition.

Mechanism
Two mechanisms are mixed and the company does not split them. (1) AI-driven hyperpersonalisation of offers and pricing against a 93.4m-subscriber base - genuine deployment, and the reading that would make this a real S2. (2) Bundling third-party AI subscriptions (Gemini, Adobe Express) into mobile plans as paid add-ons - that is distribution of somebody else's AI, closer to a reseller margin than to ROI on Indosat's own AI spend. The disclosed ARPU number covers both.
Why this shape
S2, not S3. Test (a): absent the AI, these subscribers were already buying mobile data from Indosat or a rival - the counterfactual purchase plainly exists. Test (b): the prior alternative was a cheaper plan from the same carrier or from Telkomsel/XLSmart, so any share gain came out of a competitor or out of the customer's own downgrade decision. Zero-sum at the Indonesian industry level and GDP-neutral: this is monetisation intensity on a flat subscriber base, not new demand. Worth logging anyway because a non-tech incumbent putting a specific revenue-per-user figure next to AI on the record is rare - most of this log's S1/S2 entries stop at input metrics.
Status
ESTIMATED — company's own internal attribution
Source

Indosat Ooredoo Hutchison H1 2026 results release, 'Delivers Double-Digit Growth, Accelerating Its AI-Led Transformation' (reprinted in full by TelecomTV); corroborated by TNGlobal, 29 Jul 2026, 2026-07-29
Falsifier

OpenAI / Chatterji, Holtz, Rakholia, Tambe & Weeratunga - 'How Organizations Use AI: Evidence from ChatGPT' (1,500+ organizations, 17m+ messages, 417 US public-company tickers) Cross-sector (research) · US S2 — Share⚠ Counter-evidence

Working paper submitted 12 Aug 2026 linking ChatGPT Enterprise account records to Compustat financials for US public companies through March 2026. On the only revenue question it asks, the answer is null: "Revenue per employee is not meaningfully associated with output tokens per employee once other controls are included", and for the other usage margins "the point estimates are positive for messages per active usage week per employee and weekly active users per employee, but neither coefficient is statistically significant." Adoption runs the other way - "firms with higher revenue per employee are more likely to adopt ChatGPT Enterprise", with "a one-log-point increase in lagged revenue per employee ... associated with roughly 0.4 to 0.9 percentage points higher adoption probability."

Mechanism
Not a deployment finding - a measurement of whether the revenue signal this log hunts one company at a time shows up in the largest linked enterprise-AI usage dataset that exists. 521 ticker-year observations across 417 tickers with positive message volume, fiscal years 2024-2025.
Why this shape
Counter-evidence tagged to S2, the shape that claims AI produces attributable incremental revenue at adopting firms. Read it precisely, because the headline overstates it: revenue per employee here is LAGGED - measured before adoption - so this is a cross-sectional selection regression, not a causal test of whether AI raised anyone's revenue. What it establishes is (i) high-revenue-per-employee firms adopt more, i.e. adoption is selected on prior performance, which is exactly the endogeneity that makes every adopter-outperforms-non-adopter statistic worthless, and (ii) among adopters, usage intensity carries no measurable revenue signal. The load-bearing fact is the absence: OpenAI had ChatGPT Enterprise usage linked to Compustat financials through March 2026 - the data needed to test post-adoption revenue changes - and published 69 pages of adoption, seniority and task taxonomy without that result. The strongest-incentive party with the best dataset did not produce the number.
Status

Source

Falsifier

Mechanism

The 9.9% issued-application growth in the same quarter is credited to a "multifaceted approach" of affordability, product, distribution, marketing and claims - not to AI - which is attribution discipline by the company, and worth recording as such.

On the Q2 2026 call (6 Aug 2026) CEO Tobias Hartmann said "around 20% of active searches on Cars.com are engaging with Carson", that "Carson users are 4x more likely to submit a lead", and that Carson users "accounted for nearly 30% of total leads submitted in the month of June". Total Q2 revenue rose 1% to $179.9m; marketplace revenue rose more than 7% while OEM and national advertising fell 18%. No revenue was attributed to Carson.

Mechanism
Carson is an LLM shopping assistant inside the Cars.com marketplace. Cars.com does not sell cars; it sells lead flow and subscription packages to franchise and independent dealers. So the revenue path runs assistant engagement -> lead volume and lead quality -> dealer willingness to pay for marketplace packages (Premium Plus, the fastest-growing package in the quarter, targeted at 15% adoption by year-end). Carson sits upstream of the thing that is actually monetized, which is why the disclosed numbers are lead metrics rather than dollars.
Why this shape
S1. Every disclosed figure is an input metric - engagement rate, lead-submission propensity, share of leads - and none is revenue. Revenue conversion was NOT disclosed, and the surrounding print argues against assuming it: total revenue grew 1% in the same quarter that AI-assisted leads reportedly reached ~30% of the June total. Either the lead-quality gain has not yet repriced dealer packages, or the 4x figure is largely selection - shoppers already close to submitting a lead are the ones who engage an assistant. Cars.com offers no control group, exactly as Amazon offered none for "spend 40% more per order" (logged 12 Aug). Note also that ~30% of leads is a June figure, not a Q2 figure; the quarter-level number was not given.

Xometry Industrials / Manufacturing marketplace · US S1 — Velocity

On the Q2 2026 call (4 Aug 2026) CEO Sanjeev Sahni said "our AI models are effectively optimizing conversion, buyer growth and market share gains", disclosing an "approximately 15% improvement in CNC cost prediction accuracy" from a new-generation model and process-recommendation acceptance reaching "85%, driven by an upgraded context-aware AI process recommender". Marketplace revenue grew 45% YoY to $215.4m, total revenue 41% to a record $229.3m, active buyers 20% to 89,557; marketing spend fell to a record-low 3.4% of marketplace revenue.

Mechanism
Xometry quotes custom-manufactured parts instantly from an uploaded CAD file and routes the job to a supplier network, earning the spread. Pricing accuracy is therefore the P&L: underprice and gross margin is lost on the job, overprice and the quote does not convert. A 15% improvement in cost-prediction accuracy widens the band of jobs that can be quoted profitably, and the process recommender picking the right manufacturing process 85% of the time removes a human sourcing step. Sahni's framing that the pricing, sourcing and manufacturability models are "no longer independently doing" their jobs but "all interconnected" is the specific 2025-26 change.
Why this shape
S1. The disclosed AI numbers are input metrics - prediction accuracy and recommendation acceptance - not revenue. The 45% marketplace growth is real and management links it verbally to the models, but no dollar of it is attributed, and buyer growth of 20% against revenue growth of 45% means most of the delta is wallet share and mix, which management did not decompose. Revenue conversion is therefore asserted, not disclosed. Explicitly NOT S3: these are better quotes for parts buyers who already buy machined parts from job shops, so absent Xometry the demand exists and someone serves it - test (a) fails. Scope call: kept in the main log rather than the appendix because Xometry's revenue is the sale of manufactured parts, not software licences; it is a distributor with an ML pricing engine, not a vendor selling AI. Reasonable people could push it to the appendix, and the ML quoting engine long predates the 24-month capability frontier.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source

Second falsifier: if the 15% accuracy and 85% acceptance statistics are not restated or bettered on the Q3 call, treat them as launch marketing for the model refresh rather than managed KPIs.

Shopify E-commerce platform (out of scope - software) · CA S2 — Share

On the Q2 2026 call (5 Aug 2026) President Harley Finkelstein said "AI-driven traffic and also orders to Shopify stores tripled year-over-year in the second quarter", that new buyer orders from AI channels "are coming in at nearly twice the rate of other channels", that "AI searches powered by Catalog converted twice the rate of those using scraped data", and that "75% of AI-referred orders originated from outside the top 100 merchant categories". GMV grew 32% to $115.6bn and revenue 34% to $3.6bn. No dollar amount of revenue was attributed to AI; management called AI "small relative to our massive GMV".

Mechanism
LLM assistants answer a shopping intent in natural language and resolve it against structured product data. Shopify's Catalog exposes its merchants' inventory to those assistants in machine-readable form rather than leaving them to scrape it, and the 2x conversion gap between Catalog-sourced and scraped results is the price of that structure. Because assistants match intent rather than keywords, the products they surface skew away from the categories that win search-engine auctions.
Why this shape
Appendix: Shopify is a software platform and out of scope as a subject in the main revenue log. Classified S2, not S3, and the distinction is the whole point of retrieving this. The long-tail statistic - 75% of AI-referred orders outside the top 100 categories - is the strongest S3-shaped claim retrieved this run, and it still fails the tests. (a) Counterfactual purchase: not established; a buyer who finds a niche product through an assistant was in most cases going to buy something in that need-state from somebody. (b) Prior alternative: the prior alternative was keyword search, which resolved to head merchants and paid placement - so this reallocates demand from head to tail, and from Google-mediated discovery to assistant-mediated discovery. Someone lost those orders. (c) Capability gate: passes cleanly - intent-resolving assistants at consumer scale did not exist 24 months ago. (d) Newly economic: partially - discovery cost for long-tail SKUs collapsed - but Shopify discloses no evidence that total category demand rose rather than moved. Three of four tests are not met, so this is a distribution shift, not new TAM. Also note what is absent: after tripling, still no dollar figure, and an explicit management caveat that the base is small.

Shopify Q2 2026 earnings call transcript (Motley Fool), 2026-08-05
Falsifier
If Shopify quantifies AI-attributed GMV in dollars in a later quarter and the number is material, this entry understates the finding. Conversely, if AI-attributed traffic triples again in Q3 while total GMV growth stays in the low 30s, the channel is substituting for search rather than adding to demand - which is the S2 reading confirmed. A separate ledger: never counted in the S1–S3 revenue totals, because cost evidence is far more abundant than revenue evidence and pooling the two would bury the question this log exists to answer.
Ford Motor Company Automotive · US C3 — Non-tech · engineering⚠ Counter-evidence

Ford VP of vehicle hardware engineering Charles Poon said the company "mistakenly" believed that introducing AI and adjusting design requirements would produce a high-quality product, and that Ford has since rehired, newly hired or promoted experienced engineers into vehicle engineering to fix the resulting quality gaps - reported as approximately 300 by Fox Business (citing a Bloomberg press call) and as 350 by The Next Web (citing The Verge). Poon: "Artificial intelligence is a fantastic tool, but it's only as good as the information you use to train it." Ford subsequently topped J.D. Power's 2026 US Initial Quality Study at 152 problems per 100 vehicles.

Mechanism
Ford substituted AI-assisted design-requirement tooling for experienced engineering judgement in vehicle quality. The failure mode Poon describes is not that the models were bad but that the veterans who held the tacit knowledge left before it was captured, so the tools amplified weak inputs instead of catching design flaws. The correction was headcount - re-hiring senior engineers - plus, per the reporting, a large expansion of AI-powered edge-case testing running alongside the humans rather than instead of them.
Why this shape
C3 - non-tech company, engineering function. Counter-evidence to the cost thesis in its strongest available form: not a study, not a survey, but a named incumbent that made the substitution, measured the result, and bought the labour back. It bears on METHOD's standing trap 4 in reverse - here the headcount action has an explicitly AI-attributed cause, stated by the executive responsible, against the company's interest. Note carefully what it does and does not prove: Ford went on to lead J.D. Power initial quality, so the end state is AI plus experienced engineers beating both prior arrangements. The refutable claim is substitution, not augmentation.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier C — Third-party reporting — journalism, trade press, sell-side
Fox Business, 29 Jun 2026, reporting a Ford press call originally covered by Bloomberg; corroborated by The Next Web, 26 Jun 2026, citing The Verge, 2026-06-29
Falsifier
If Ford's engineering headcount falls back over FY2026-27 while quality rankings hold, the rehire was a transition cost of a substitution that ultimately worked, and this entry overstates the reversal. If Ford quantifies the cost of the rehire or of the quality failures - warranty, recall or campaign expense traced to the AI-designed period - the entry should be upgraded from anecdote to a measured loss.
Confidence
low — Two retrieved sources give materially different engineer counts (approximately 300 vs 350) and name different primary outlets (Bloomberg vs The Verge) for the same press call. Neither primary could be retrieved this run - theverge.com blocks this agent and the Bloomberg original is paywalled - so no single figure is verified and none is asserted here. Only Poon's quoted sentence appears verbatim in a document retrieved this run. The item is also roughly seven weeks old, outside the normal window, and is logged on the METHOD ground that counter-evidence decays slowly and this one was unlogged.

Amazon Retail / Cloud · US S1 — Velocity⚠ Counter-evidence

On the Q2 2026 call (30 Jul 2026) CEO Andy Jassy said of Alexa for Shopping, Amazon's agentic shopping assistant, that "Over 350 million customers have used it in the last 12 months, and engagement accelerated in Q2, with active users nearly doubling and interactions up over 5x year-over-year", that in the U.S. "customers who use Alexa for Shopping spend an average of over 40% more per order than those who don't", and that "customers who've tried Alexa+ are signing up for Prime at nearly 25% higher rates". Mechanism
An agentic shopping assistant giving personalised recommendations, product comparisons and price history, and automating purchase through price alerts and auto-buy, sitting in front of the retail catalogue; Alexa+ as the household entry point feeding Prime signup.
Why this shape
S1, not S2: the disclosed number is a cohort spend differential - an input/behavioural metric - not attributed incremental revenue. Selection bias is unaddressed and fatal to the naive reading, since engaged, high-frequency, high-spend shoppers are exactly the ones most likely to use an assistant; Amazon offers no counterfactual. Appendix scope because Amazon is a hyperscaler and out of scope as a subject in the main revenue log, but it belongs in the appendix as the sharpest available comparator: nobody in the main log has a consumer-facing AI cohort metric at this scale. The S1 standing caveat is the whole point of the entry - revenue conversion is conspicuously absent, and it was previously present. Per Modern Retail (Tier C, retrieved this run) Amazon disclosed at its February 2026 earnings that Rufus "was used by 300 million customers in 2025 and helped drive nearly $12 billion in incremental annualized sales", and Fortune (Nov 2025) quotes Jassy saying Rufus "is expected to generate over $10 billion in annual incremental sales for us". The word 'Rufus' does not appear anywhere in the Q2 2026 transcript and no dollar attribution replaces it. Confound stated plainly: no source retrieved this run confirms that Alexa for Shopping is a rebranded Rufus, so the metric basis may have changed rather than the disclosure being withdrawn.

Hims can tell you tasks fell by half, and hands a 40% revenue quarter to GLP-1s and an acquisition. Companies measure AI where it touches their own cost base because that is where the instrumentation already existed.
A second, sharper tell: executives are starting to pre-commit to payback dates instead of results. Hims' CFO gave "12 to 18 months"; Hamilton's CFO said "the operational benefits and those productivity gains have to exceed what the technology expenses are as well." Both are honest, and both are admissions that the return is not in the numbers yet. Those are dated, falsifiable checkpoints - the log should hold them to it.

  • Hamilton Insurance Group - fails bar 2. No submission count, hit ratio or quote volume.   • Expedia Group (call 5 Aug) - consumer-facing generative features "not driving conversion yet"; what they produce is "over 60% more information about traveler intent." Logged S2 counter-evidence.
  • Zillow Group (call 5 Aug) - AI mode users contact an agent at "nearly three times the rate," at 20% of signed-in users; $772m revenue +18% not attributed to it. Logged S1 at low confidence - uncontrolled, self-selected.
  • Allstate (call 6 Aug) - built the entire call narrative on ALLIE, "over 250 analytical models and 40 petabytes of data," said it "should positively impact all areas" and that "Agentic AI will connect our sophisticated capabilities to improve growth effectiveness." Zero quantified revenue, policy, conversion or quote figures. Not logged.
  • AIG (call 7 Aug) - "our underwriters are reviewing more submissions and generating quotes significantly faster." Exactly the right metric with no number on it, framed against a sub-30% expense-ratio target. Not logged.
The pattern, now visible across three consecutive weeks: the AI-to-revenue bridge keeps getting built from the input side and never finished. Skyward quantifies submission speed and hands premium growth to business mix. Zillow quantifies engagement and hands revenue to nothing. AIG says "more" and "significantly faster" and points at the expense ratio. Allstate says everything and quantifies nothing. Four insurers and marketplaces in one week produced input metrics and expense ratios; not one produced attributed premium or attributed revenue.
Second pattern, and it needs naming before it contaminates the log: "AI revenue" at non-tech incumbents is increasingly AI-capex revenue. Caterpillar (Q2, 4 Aug) posted power generation retail sales +72% on data-centre demand; Siemens booked record orders with triple-digit data-centre growth; Deutsche Telekom's Munich industrial AI cloud is "fully sold out with 10,000 GPUs." All three are non-tech corporations earning real money from AI - as suppliers to the AI build-out, selling SKUs with long predecessors in their own catalogues. That is AI-as-customer, not AI-as-capability, and it is not what this log measures. None logged.

Near-misses deliberately not logged

  • Allstate - fails inclusion bar 2. Narrative without a number; management said it could not provide specific figures. An explicit refusal to quantify is informative but is not a null result.
  • AIG - fails inclusion bar 2. "More submissions" and "significantly faster" are unquantified.
  • Instacart (6 Aug) - CEO Chris Rogers, in prepared remarks: "Orders placed with our AI assistant are, on average, larger than our typical basket." Directionally the right claim and a primary source, but no magnitude, and the CFO attributed the 4% AOV rise to club retailers and engagement, explicitly not to AI. Fails bar 2.
  • Group 1 Automotive (call 30 Jul) - a secondary summary reported virtual F&I agents processing 7-10 deals per day versus roughly 3 for in-store managers. I fetched the transcript and that comparison is not in it. What management actually said is "in those stores, 20% of our F&I volume is virtual" - an adoption metric, not throughput. Rejected on verification, and recorded here because the near-miss is the method working.
  • Caterpillar / Siemens / Deutsche Telekom - AI-as-customer, per the callout above.
  • Tripadvisor (6 Aug) - "While AI-driven traffic remains small today." No number, revenue missed. Fails bar 2.
  • Wendy's (7 Aug) - no mention of AI or FreshAI anywhere in the transcript retrieved, against -12.5% US same-restaurant traffic. A previously touted AI initiative absent from disclosure would be a genuine finding under follow-up discipline, but Wendy's was never in this log and an absence-of-mention in a single aggregator rendering is not evidence. Flagged for the next run.
  • Colombia smallholder parametric crop cover (Swiss Re, Munich Re, AXA Climate, La Previsora; US$20.14m limit, 14,402 farmers; 7 Aug) - textbook S3 shape, a segment previously uneconomic to serve. Rejected: no AI. The trigger is a satellite water-balance index; AI is not mentioned. Also donor-subsidised, so willingness-to-pay is not the binding constraint.
  • CMS code G0680 - $15.50 for AI analysis of coronary and aortic valve calcium on chest CTs already taken. This is the purest available example of the S3 mechanism, diagnostics newly billable at a price point that previously supported no service. No company has disclosed revenue against it, and the source predates the window.

Nearest dated catalysts

  • HeartFlow (HTFL) Q2 2026 - 13 Aug. The most likely place a revenue number attaches to AI-enabled cardiac imaging, i.e. the G0680 mechanism above. Highest-value check on the calendar.
  • Deere & Co Q3 FY26 - 20 Aug. Autonomy-as-subscription is the cleanest incumbent S3 thesis in industrials; nothing has been quantified yet.
  • Walmart, Target, Home Depot, Lowe's Q2 - mid to late Aug. Retail personalisation and retail media are where an S2 conversion number is most likely to be volunteered.
  • Wendy's - confirm against the 8-K whether FreshAI has genuinely dropped out of disclosure.
  • Wyndham Q3 - whether the 20% LLM conversion premium and the per-franchisee $100,000 figure survive contact with Booking's sub-1% room-night reading.
Booking Holdings Travel / Hospitality · US S2 — Share⚠ Counter-evidence

On the Q2 2026 call (4 Aug 2026) CFO Ewout Steenbergen said "traffic from large language models remains below 1% of room nights," adding it "hasn't moved so much recently" and represents "no material change over the last few months or quarters." Asked to quantify conversion or satisfaction gains from Priceline's agentic assistant Penny, CEO Glenn Fogel declined: "it's still very small, and we're not at a stage we're going to start giving away percentages." The only quantified AI benefit disclosed was cost - customer service cost per booking falling at a double-digit rate - against AI expense running at "a low single-digit level of our overall technology spend."

Mechanism
None - this is the absence of a mechanism. The claimed path (guests discover and book travel inside an LLM, so whoever wins the agentic channel wins the demand) is measured here at the point where it would show up in the P&L, room nights, and it is under 1% and flat.
Why this shape
Counter-evidence tagged to S2. It contradicts the channel-capture thesis logged for Wyndham on 2026-08-02, where CEO Geoff Ballotti said "roughly 60% of travel searches by our guests are occurring within an LLM" with "a 20% higher conversion on our brand.com sites when the guest connects to us from an LLM." These are not formally the same metric - searches by one franchisor's guests is not room nights across the world's largest OTA - but they are the same claim measured at two points, and the harder number is the small one. The mismatch is the finding: the LLM channel is loud upstream in search and negligible downstream in bookings. Note also that Booking is the incumbent with the most to lose from agentic disintermediation and therefore an incentive to describe the channel as small; that cuts against the disclosure, and is why the flat trend statement matters more than the level.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier A — Primary and accountable — filing, transcript, formal guidance
Q2 2026 earnings call (transcript via Investing.com), 2026-08-04
Falsifier
If Q3 or Q4 2026 shows LLM-originated room nights crossing 1% and accelerating, or Booking starts disclosing agentic conversion percentages it refused to give here, the channel is real and this entry is the wrong read. Conversely, if the sub-1% figure quietly stops being disclosed while agentic partnerships are still being announced, that is the tell in the other direction.
Confidence
medium — Tier A call, CFO on the record, a specific bound on the number - but verified via a transcript aggregator rather than Booking's own filing, and "below 1% of room nights" is a ceiling, not a measurement.
Expedia Group Travel / Hospitality · US S2 — Share⚠ Counter-evidence

On the Q2 2026 call (5 Aug 2026) CEO Ariane Gorin described Vrbo's natural-language search and Property Expert agent as developments that are not driving conversion yet, saying of them: "what we're finding is that you get over 60% more information about traveler intent, and that allows us to deepen the relationships with the traveler." She drew an explicit line between AI that pays now - ranking, recommendations, personalising the UX - and consumer-facing generative features whose benefit is framed as compounding over time rather than realised.

Mechanism
The claimed S2 mechanism - a conversational front end converts browsers into bookers better than a search box - is being measured by Expedia and is not showing up in conversion. What it does produce is intent data, an input to future ranking and personalisation, which is a bet on a later conversion gain rather than a present one.
Why this shape
Counter-evidence tagged to S2. This is the most useful kind of disclosure in this log: a CEO with every incentive to claim an AI conversion win, in the quarter she raised outlook, volunteering that the flagship consumer AI features are not converting. The "over 60% more information about traveler intent" figure is a denominator game by METHOD's own definition - more data collected is not revenue - and she does not present it as one. Note the countervailing detail: the same coverage says AI-powered personalisation helped drive "record attach rates" for the Expedia brand with no number attached, so the company is not disowning AI, it is separating the part that works from the part that does not.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier C — Third-party reporting — journalism, trade press, sell-side
PhocusWire, "Expedia Group is investing in AI that doesn't convert yet", 2026-08-05
Falsifier
If Q3 or Q4 2026 discloses a conversion lift from natural-language search or Property Expert, this reads as an early-stage timing statement rather than a null. If instead these features stop being discussed while intent-data framing persists, the null hardened.
Confidence
medium — The "over 60% more information about traveler intent" clause is verbatim-confirmed in the trade-press rendering I fetched; the "not driving conversion" characterisation is that outlet's reported speech, not a verbatim sentence I could confirm. I have not read the primary transcript, so this is logged Tier C rather than A.
Skyward Specialty Insurance Group Insurance · US S1 — Velocity

On the Q2 2026 call (5 Aug 2026) CEO Andrew Robinson described "bionic underwriting" - "this idea of being able to automate the ingestion of everything that is submission related" - and disclosed "40% faster submissions to underwriters, 35% improvement in speed to quote." He added that "half of our underwriting is benefiting from machine learning and predictive analytics," and described SkyScore, which "allows us to ingest all financial information without any human intervention to score every principal on 10 different dimensions" in Surety.

Mechanism
Submission intake in E&S is the binding constraint on premium: an underwriter can only quote what reaches the desk in a usable form. Automating ingestion of broker submissions raises the number of risks an underwriter sees per day and shortens time-to-quote, which in a broker-intermediated market raises the probability of being the first quote back and therefore the hit ratio. SkyScore extends the same logic into Surety by machine-scoring the principal's financials rather than queueing them for a human credit analyst.
Why this shape
S1: every disclosed number is an input metric - submission speed, speed to quote, share of underwriting touched by ML. No premium dollar is attributed to any of it. Revenue conversion is CONSPICUOUSLY ABSENT and the absence is unusually clean: gross written premium grew 13% year over year on the same call and management attributed that growth to business mix and market diversification (A&H, Agriculture, Credit & Surety) and to underwriting discipline - not to AI. The only financial line management did connect to AI was the expense ratio, which improved 2.7 points to 24.3%. Per METHOD's one-disclosure-one-ledger rule this is logged once, in the revenue ledger, because the 40%/35% throughput figures are what the entry is about; the expense-ratio reading is noted here rather than double-logged as C4.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier A — Primary and accountable — filing, transcript, formal guidance
Q2 2026 earnings call (transcript via Investing.com), 2026-08-05
Falsifier
If Q3 and Q4 2026 repeat the throughput percentages without ever disclosing a hit ratio, a submission-count-to-bound-premium conversion, or premium growth management is willing to attribute to underwriting technology, then bionic underwriting is an expense-ratio story wearing a growth story's clothes. Specifically: watch whether the 40%/35% figures reappear alongside a quote or bind volume, or whether they migrate permanently into the expense-ratio discussion.
Confidence
medium — Named CEO, on the record, Tier A call, hard percentages - but I verified the wording against a transcript aggregator rather than Skyward's own audio or 8-K, and the numbers are management's own internal measurement with no stated baseline or methodology.
Zillow Group Real estate · US S1 — Velocity

On the Q2 2026 call (5 Aug 2026) CEO Jeremy Wacksman said: "Consumers who use AI mode spend more than three times as long on Zillow, view more than twice as many homes, run nearly three times as many searches - and contact an agent at nearly three times the rate of consumers who don't use AI mode." AI mode is live for roughly 20% of signed-in users. Q2 revenue was $772m, up 18%, and none of it was attributed to AI mode.

Mechanism
Agent contacts are the unit Zillow sells: Premier Agent revenue is a function of connection volume and price per connection. A conversational search mode that triples the contact rate per user is, mechanically, a Premier Agent inventory expansion - if the effect is causal.
Why this shape
S1: every number is an engagement input, none is revenue. Revenue conversion is conspicuously absent - 18% revenue growth was not tied to AI mode, and no connection-volume dollar impact was given. The severe caveat, which management did not address: this is a self-selection statistic. The consumer who opts into AI mode is the high-intent consumer who was always going to contact an agent. No holdout, no control, no causal claim was offered. Logged at low confidence for exactly that reason, and it is the cleanest current example of the AI engagement metric that looks like a revenue mechanism and may be a sorting artefact.
Status
ESTIMATED — company's own internal attribution
Source
Tier C — Third-party reporting — journalism, trade press, sell-side
HousingWire, Zillow Q2 2026 earnings (call 5 Aug 2026), 2026-08-05
Falsifier
If Zillow rolls AI mode past ~20% of signed-in users toward the full base and the 3x contact-rate multiple collapses toward 1x, the original figure was selection, not lift. Equally, if Premier Agent connection volume or revenue per visitor never inflects while AI-mode penetration triples, the mechanism does not convert.
Confidence
low — The quote is verbatim-confirmed across two independent renderings, but the statistic is uncontrolled and self-selected, and the marketplace's own revenue growth was pointedly not attributed to it. A 3x with no holdout is not evidence of a 3x effect.

Neither will say what it earned. That silence is the finding — an incumbent that had a revenue figure worth citing would cite it.

  • Agentic-commerce traffic statistics (Adobe, Salesforce aggregates) — no named deploying company, and referred-traffic growth is a denominator game, not incremental revenue.

2026-08-03

permalink →

Assessment

No revenue-side finding cleared the bar today and no S3 appeared - the fourth consecutive run without one. What did clear the bar is the sharpest version yet of the pattern this log exists to detect: on a single day, 30 Jul 2026, three non-tech incumbents put precise numbers next to AI cost, and the only party that put a number next to AI revenue was the software vendor selling to them.

Sources read

  • WTW - Q2 2026 call, 30 Jul. Programme 'Propel': $625m of cash spend to produce $400m of run-rate savings by end-2028, path to a 30% enterprise operating margin. Logged C4. Revenue leg explicitly not quantified - CFO Krasner: "the growth comes from how we redeploy that capacity that gets generated over time."
  • TP (Teleperformance) - H1 2026 results, 30 Jul. 1,100+ AI projects in production, 2.3x YoY; EUR 150-170m run-rate savings programme with roughly EUR 50-70m verbally attributed to AI in core operations. Logged C4. EBITDA margin flat YoY - nothing has landed yet.
  • The Baldwin Insurance Group - Q2 2026 call, 30 Jul. Direct bill reconciliation run-rate cost $3m to $1m, internal labour $1.2m to $400k, quality 90% to 98%. Logged C4, and the cleanest realized cost entry in the ledger so far - named process, before-and-after dollars, no composite.
  • CCC Intelligent Solutions - Q2 2026 call, 30 Jul. $120m annualized AI-solution revenue, ~50% YoY growth, 11% of total revenue, 4 points of company growth. Logged S2, appendix - software vendor, out of scope as a revenue subject.
  • Hyatt - Q2 2026 call, 30 Jul. Read in full for AI revenue attribution; none quantified.
  • Standard Chartered - H1 2026 results, 29 Jul. Checked the results release directly for AI language after third-party coverage implied it. There is none.
The asymmetry is the finding. CCC will tell you AI is 11% of its revenue. The "top five insurers" deploying that same AI are named only as "top five insurers", and have disclosed nothing. Every quantified AI revenue number available this week sits one layer up the value chain from the company actually using the AI.

Worth naming a second-order pattern across the three cost entries, because it cuts against the loudest version of the cost thesis: all three companies declined to bank a revenue consequence, and two of them said so explicitly. WTW routes the benefit through capacity redeployment. Baldwin says the benefit is "embedded in the numbers". This is METHOD trap 2 showing up not as one CEO's caution but as a sector convention in insurance services.

And the magnitudes deserve stating plainly. Baldwin's disclosure is the honest one and it is about 15 basis points of revenue. WTW's is $400m but costs $625m to get and does not arrive until 2028. Neither is a margin event yet.

Near-misses deliberately not logged

  • Hyatt - has "a large-scale AI platform to actually score and value every piece of group business" and an AI platform targeting "revenue opportunities not costs". Fails inclusion bar 2: no number attached to either. The 40% PMS cost reduction they did quantify is a platform migration, not AI.
  • Yum! Brands / Byte by Yum - checked as a candidate incumbent AI SKU with no predecessor in its own catalog. The available disclosure is Q4 2025 (Feb 2026), already stale, and quantifies stockouts and ordering failures rather than revenue or third-party licensing. No S3 counterfactual established.
  • Standard Chartered - the 7,800-role reduction attributed to AI is from May 2026, is headcount with several plausible parents (METHOD trap 4), and the H1 2026 results release contains no AI attribution at all. That silence is more interesting than the May headline and is worth re-checking at FY results.
  • TP's EUR 15-20m H2 2026 financial-services win - real and dated, but it is TP selling AI-enabled services. Shovel revenue, out of scope as a revenue subject; noted inside the TP cost entry rather than double-logged.
  • Armilla / Klaimee and the AI-liability insurance market - the most promising live S3 hunting ground and searched again today. Everything found is either an AI-native MGA with no disclosed premium volume or vendor-adjacent market commentary. No named carrier has disclosed written premium on an AI-risk class. This remains the single best place for a genuine S3 to appear and it has not yet.

Nearest dated catalysts

  • Yum! Brands Q2 2026 - due early Aug. The Byte licensing question is whether any third-party operator revenue is disclosed for the first time.
  • John Deere Q3 FY26 - mid-Aug. Autonomy-as-subscription is the cleanest incumbent S3 test available; watch for a per-acre or per-machine recurring number rather than an equipment sale.
  • Walmart, Target, Home Depot Q2 - mid-to-late Aug. Retail media and personalization are the highest-probability S2 disclosures left this season.
  • WTW FY2026 - first checkpoint on whether any of the $400m starts appearing.
  • Standard Chartered FY2026 - whether the May AI-attributed headcount action ever gets a financial number attached to it.

No findings cleared the bar for the main revenue log. 1 out-of-scope finding in the appendix and 3 in the cost ledger below. Angles run: S3: insurers pricing previously uninsurable / declined risk classes, S3: AI liability and AI-agent insurance - named carriers, written premium, S3: pharma/biotech previously unscreenable target or indication space, S3: industrials selling autonomy as a new subscription line (ag, mining, construction), S3: healthcare diagnostics/monitoring at newly viable price points, S3: professional services work never billable because delivery cost exceeded willingness to pay, S3: incumbent AI-enabled SKU with no predecessor in its own catalog (Yum! Appendix — revenue findings of any shape whose subject is out of scope (software / AI-native). Kept visible so the S3 picture is not artificially empty and the incumbent-vs-entrant split stays measurable. Not counted in the revenue totals above.
CCC Intelligent Solutions Software - insurance claims · US S2 — Share

On the Q2 2026 call (30 Jul 2026) CCC disclosed "over $120 million of annualized revenue from AI-based solutions that are growing at nearly 50% year-over-year", representing "approximately 11% of total revenue in the second quarter and growing approximately 45% year-over-year", and said "revenue from AI-based solutions contributed 4 points of growth", driven by APD, subrogation and EvolutionIQ. Named deployments: "a top five insurer became its largest carrier to date to adopt the AI-powered subrogation solution", and "two top five insurers expanded their use of AI-enabled claims workflows by deploying the First Look solution to identify total losses earlier".

Mechanism
Computer-vision damage appraisal and AI claims workflow sold as add-on modules into CCC's existing installed base of auto insurers and collision repairers - subrogation demand packaging, early total-loss identification, and medical-claim synthesis via the acquired EvolutionIQ technology.
Why this shape
Appendix: the subject is a software vendor, out of scope for the main revenue log - CCC's AI revenue is its own sales, not adoption ROI at the deploying carriers. Shape S2, not S3, and the distinction is the point: these are add-on modules sold into an installed base that already buys CCC's claims platform, and the work displaced was either a competitor's tool or the carrier's own unpaid manual staff time. Test (a) fails - absent CCC's AI the carriers would still be processing subrogation and total losses, and paying somebody to do it. Test (c) is arguable for the vision models but not established by the source. The genuinely useful reading is the asymmetry: the quantified AI revenue sits at the vendor, and the carriers deploying it - all named only as 'top five insurers' - have disclosed no corresponding revenue or loss-ratio number of their own. That is the shape of this entire log in one disclosure.

Falsifier
If the ~50% YoY growth in AI-solution revenue decelerates toward CCC's ~7-8% overall growth rate within four quarters, the AI line was a re-labelling of existing modules rather than a new attach. Separately: if any named top-five carrier later discloses a quantified claims-cost or cycle-time benefit, this entry becomes the vendor half of a matched pair and the deploying-side number can finally be tested.
Confidence
high — Tier A, specific, dated, repeated across two framings (dollar and percentage) that reconcile with each other; the only softness is that 'AI-based solutions' is a company-defined revenue category.
Cost ledger — AI-attributed cost savings and productivity gains, on a 2×2 of sector (tech / non-tech) × function (software engineering / everything else). Tech subjects are in scope here, unlike the revenue log. A separate ledger: never counted in the S1–S3 revenue totals, because cost evidence is far more abundant than revenue evidence and pooling the two would bury the question this log exists to answer.
WTW (Willis Towers Watson) Insurance broking / advisory · US / UK C4 — Non-tech · non-engineering

On the Q2 2026 call (30 Jul 2026) WTW launched programme 'Propel', committing to spend "approximately $625 million in cash" plus "approximately $25 million in non-cash charges to produce approximately $400 million in run-rate savings" by the end of 2028, from "embedding AI and automation across our business" (CEO Carl Hess). CFO Andrew Krasner tied it to "2028 adjusted operating margin targets of 30% at the enterprise, 35% in HWC, and 30% in R&B". No revenue uplift was quantified: asked where growth comes from, Krasner said "the growth comes from how we redeploy that capacity that gets generated over time."

Mechanism
Broking and HWC workflow automation - the named live tools are Navigator and Neuron, described as "not pilots, they're live and in daily use" - plus process automation across a labour-heavy advisory cost base. The saving is headcount and process cost in client-facing and support functions, not engineering.
Why this shape
C4: non-tech (insurance broking and HR/benefits advisory), non-engineering function - the cost base being attacked is broking and consulting labour. Not a revenue shape at any point: WTW quantified the spend, the saving and the margin target, and explicitly declined to quantify a revenue uplift, routing it instead through capacity redeployment. That is METHOD trap 2 in partial form - management banks the cost saving in guidance but leaves the revenue leg entirely qualitative. Note also the ratio: $625m of cash out to get $400m of annual run-rate saving is a ~1.6x payback-year gross, i.e. the run-cost offset is being paid up front and disclosed, which is rarer and better than most cost claims in this ledger.
Status

Confidence

TP (Teleperformance SE) Business services / BPO · France C4 — Non-tech · non-engineering

In H1 2026 results (30 Jul 2026) TP disclosed "the number of AI projects running in production reaching 1,100+ in Q2 2026", up 2.3x, with 272 new AI projects launched in Q2 alone, and said "the AI and efficiency program is expected to generate annual run-rate savings of EUR 150-170 million, with half of these benefits to be realized in 2026." EBITDA margin was flat year over year.

Mechanism
AI applied to TP's own internal delivery cost base - candidate screening and recruiting, automated QA of agent interactions, and workforce/schedule management across a several-hundred-thousand-person delivery workforce.
Why this shape
C4: non-tech (business process outsourcing), non-engineering function. Logged once, in the cost ledger, per the single-ledger rule: TP also disclosed a financial-services win projected at "EUR 15-20 million in revenue during H2 2026" and said strategic lines are "more than 15% of Core Services revenue", but that is TP selling AI-enabled services - shovel revenue, out of scope as a revenue subject - and the number the disclosure is actually about is the saving. Two standing traps apply: the 1,100 projects figure is an adoption metric, not a cost metric (trap 1), and the EUR 150-170m is a composite 'AI and efficiency' programme from which management carved out an AI share only verbally (trap 3). The Baldwin Insurance Group Insurance distribution · US C4 — Non-tech · non-engineering

2026-08-02

Assessment

Two revenue findings, both from the hospitality and specialty-insurance tape, and no S3 for the eighth consecutive run. Nothing found this run creates demand that did not previously exist; both revenue entries are demand being captured from somebody else.

Sources read

  • Wyndham Hotels & Resorts (Q2 2026 call, 23 Jul) - the most quantified non-tech AI revenue disclosure in weeks: 60% of guest travel searches now occur inside an LLM, 20% higher conversion on brand.com from LLM-originated traffic, 15% ADR premium on autonomously booked rooms, $100,000+ of attributed incremental revenue for the most engaged franchisees. Logged S2 - the guest was always going to book a room somewhere.
  • Ryan Specialty (Q2 2026 call, 30 Jul) - 11% more quotes per head over 12 months, attributed to automated data extraction and rating pre-population; FAC submissions priced "in minutes, not days". Logged S1; revenue conversion conspicuously absent, and management partly credited "hustle".
  • BIS WP 1325 - backlog entry, not fresh. 4% labour-productivity gain from AI adoption across 12,000+ EU/US non-financial firms, causally identified. Logged C4 as the magnitude check the cost ledger was missing.
  • Cigna (Q2 2026, 30 Jul), Chevron, AbbVie, Regeneron, AXIS Capital, Selective Insurance, TELUS, Accor - read, nothing that clears the bar.
Pattern across eight runs: the revenue evidence that does arrive is consistently channel capture - winning the booking, the quote, the subscription that a competitor or a cheaper channel would otherwise have had. That is real firm-level alpha and it is not economic growth. The S3 column stays empty not because nobody claims new TAM but because every claim collapses under test (a).

Near-misses deliberately not logged

  • Wiley - $49m of AI licensing revenue in FY2026, +23%, lifetime past $110m, anchored by IQVIA and OpenEvidence. Superficially the cleanest S3 available: a revenue line with no predecessor in the catalogue, buyers who did not exist as buyers three years ago. Rejected on mission, not on shape - this is revenue from selling inputs to the AI boom, not ROI on Wiley's own AI spend. Counting it would make this log an index of AI-exposure beneficiaries, which is a different and much easier question. Same reasoning excludes utilities selling power to data centres and insurers writing data-centre construction risk. Flagged here because it is a recurring temptation and the log should be explicit about why it says no.
  • Accor (H1 2026, 30 Jul) - CEO Bazin says owners are looking for "cost savings of up to 20% in the next 12 to 18 months through AI automation" and that there is "significant savings and RevPAR to be gained". A forward hope with no number attached to a result. Fails the inclusion bar on point 2.
  • Cigna - "AI tools to save customers $200 million in medical expenses over three years". Projected, three-year, and it is a customer benefit claim rather than an expense line of Cigna's that fell. Not logged in either ledger.
  • Mastercard / Visa agentic commerce - still no attributed revenue, only product architecture. Unchanged from the 1 Aug sweep.
  • Wyndham transcript discrepancy - Investing.com's slide summary and Investing.com's own transcript of the same call report materially different AI figures (4 million interactions vs 40 million cumulative messages and 260,000/day), and Benzinga's transcript of the call contains none of them. Logged with the transcript figures and the conflict recorded, rather than dropped - but this is a reminder that secondary transcript renderings are not interchangeable.

Nearest dated catalysts

  • Caterpillar, Deere and Corteva Q2/Q3 prints (early-to-mid Aug) - the standing test of whether an industrial incumbent will price autonomy as a new subscription line rather than a feature.
  • Marriott (4 Aug) and Hilton - direct comparators for Wyndham's LLM-conversion and autonomous-booking numbers. If the LLM channel is real, more than one lodging brand will quantify it this quarter.
  • News Corp, Thomson Reuters, Ziff Davis (early Aug) - more AI licensing disclosures, which will keep testing the Wiley boundary above.
  • Q3 calls late Oct - first chance to check the Wyndham falsifier and whether the per-franchisee figure survives.
Wyndham Hotels & Resorts Hospitality · US S2 — Share

On the Q2 2026 call (23 Jul 2026) CEO Geoff Ballotti said "roughly 60% of travel searches by our guests are occurring within an LLM" and disclosed "a 20% higher conversion on our brand.com sites when the guest connects to us from an LLM." Wyndham Connect, the AI guest-engagement platform, is in over 5,000 hotels handling "260,000 guest interactions via this AI tool per day" and generates "$100,000 or more in increased revenues" for engaged franchisees; Wyndham AI Concierge, live in 1,500 hotels, delivers "up to ... 500 basis points of direct contribution" and a "15% increase in ADR, if you're booking it autonomously versus on the phone."

Mechanism
Two distinct paths. (1) Demand capture: as guest search migrates into LLMs, Wyndham converts LLM-originated traffic to brand.com at a 20% higher rate than other sources - it is winning the direct booking rather than losing the guest to an OTA. (2) Autonomous booking: the AI Concierge takes voice/SMS reservations end to end at the property, and a room booked autonomously prices 15% above the same booking taken on the phone, because the agent upsells and does not drop calls. The per-franchisee revenue figure is Wyndham's attribution for the most engaged properties, not a rolled-up corporate number.
Why this shape
S2, not S3. Test: absent the AI, would this guest have booked a hotel room? Almost certainly yes - from an OTA, from a competing brand, or from the same property over the phone at a lower ADR. The mechanism is channel capture and price realisation on demand that already existed. Who lost the sale: OTAs (Booking, Expedia), competing economy/midscale brands, and Wyndham's own lower-yielding phone channel. Zero-sum at industry level and GDP-neutral - a guest who was going to sleep somewhere sleeps somewhere slightly more expensive. The 60%-of-searches-in-an-LLM figure is a channel-shift statistic, not a demand-creation one, and should not be read as new TAM.
Status
ESTIMATED — company's own internal attribution
Source
Tier A — Primary and accountable — filing, transcript, formal guidance
Q2 2026 earnings call (transcript via Investing.com), 2026-07-23
Falsifier
If Q3/Q4 2026 calls repeat the per-franchisee "$100,000 or more" and the 15% ADR premium without ever converting them into a disclosed corporate fee-revenue contribution, a RevPAR index gain against the competitive set, or a direct-vs-OTA channel mix shift, this was a franchisee sales pitch rather than a company result. Also watch whether the ADR premium survives contact with the full 5,000-hotel base rather than the 1,500 Concierge properties.
Confidence
medium — Named exec, on the record, on a Tier A call, with several hard numbers - but the revenue figure is a per-property "or more" attribution with no methodology, disclosed in the same quarter Wyndham missed on revenue. Two secondary renderings of the same disclosure disagree on detail (Investing.com's slide summary reports "4 million interactions" and "400bps customer satisfaction / 500bps voice conversion" where the transcript says 40m cumulative messages, 260k/day and "500 basis points of direct contribution"); I have used the transcript wording and flag the discrepancy. Benzinga's rendering of the same call contains none of these figures at all, which suggests at least one transcript is partial.
Ryan Specialty Holdings Insurance (specialty distribution / underwriting managers) · US S1 — Velocity

On the Q2 2026 call (30 Jul 2026) Miles Wuller, CEO of Underwriting Managers, said "the average RSUM property employee ... achieved 11% more quotes per head in the last 12 months than the prior year", attributing it to "our investments in automated data extraction, data structuring, enrichment, and rating pre-population coming to life." CEO Tim Turner said the "reinsurance FAC workbench now turns a submission into a priced decision-ready file in minutes, not days", that the firm has "started rolling out a proprietary engine for deploying AI around the firm", and that the underwriting-managers business is "auditing five times as many files as we did last year."

Mechanism
Submission intake is the bottleneck in specialty underwriting: a broker sends an unstructured schedule of values, and a human keys it into a rating engine. Automated extraction, structuring, enrichment and rating pre-population removes that step, so the same headcount quotes more accounts. Capacity, not price - Wuller's framing is explicitly that efficiency is a countervailing force against a 25-35% decline in catastrophe pricing.
Why this shape
S1: every disclosed number is an input metric - quotes per head, cycle time, files audited. Revenue conversion was conspicuously NOT disclosed. Management said the unlocked capacity "is being directed back into what matters most, winning new business" and that "high retention and new-business wins limited the decline in property revenue", but attached no AI-attributed revenue number to either. More quotes is not more bound premium: in a softening property market a higher quote count can simply mean more declined quotes at inadequate rates. Note also the honesty of the attribution - Wuller conceded the gain "certainly includes hustle", and the named mechanisms (data extraction, rating pre-population) are automation more than frontier AI, so the capability gate is weak even though the firm frames it inside its AI rollout.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier A — Primary and accountable — filing, transcript, formal guidance
Q2 2026 earnings call (transcript via Investing.com); corroborated by Coverager, 31 Jul 2026, 2026-07-30
Falsifier
If organic revenue growth in Underwriting Managers does not outpace the segment's headcount growth over the next two to three quarters, the 11% quote-per-head gain was absorbed by rate declines and lost quotes rather than converted into premium. A second tell: if the quotes-per-head metric is not repeated next year, it was a one-off talking point.
Confidence
medium — Tier A call, named executive, precise figure, and independently reported by Coverager the following day with identical wording. Marked down because the metric is self-defined ("quotes per head" over an unstated denominator of property employees), the speaker himself partly attributes it to effort rather than tooling, and no revenue conversion is offered.
Cost ledger — AI-attributed cost savings and productivity gains, on a 2×2 of sector (tech / non-tech) × function (software engineering / everything else). Tech subjects are in scope here, unlike the revenue log. A separate ledger: never counted in the S1–S3 revenue totals, because cost evidence is far more abundant than revenue evidence and pooling the two would bury the question this log exists to answer.
BIS Working Paper 1325 - 'AI adoption, productivity and employment: evidence from European firms' (12,000+ non-financial EU/US firms) Cross-sector (research) · EU / US C4 — Non-tech · non-engineering

Instrumental-variables study of matched EIBIS-ORBIS data on "more than 12,000 non-financial firms in the European Union (EU) and United States (US)", instrumenting EU firms' AI adoption with the adoption rates of matched US peers: "AI adoption increases the level of labor productivity by 4%." The authors find "no adverse effects on firm-level employment", attribute the gain to capital deepening, and find the benefit concentrated in medium and large firms.

Mechanism
Not a deployment finding - a causal estimate of the effect size the rest of this ledger is trying to measure one company at a time. Uses matched US peer adoption as an instrument to break the endogeneity between being a good firm and adopting AI, which is the flaw in every cross-sectional adoption-vs-performance correlation.
Why this shape
C4: sample is non-financial firms, effect measured at the whole-firm level rather than in an engineering function. Logged as cost/productivity, not revenue - the outcome is labor productivity, and the paper explicitly attributes it to capital deepening rather than to new demand, so nothing here supports S1-S3. Its value is as a magnitude check: 4% is one to two orders of magnitude below the 30-40% self-reported gains sitting elsewhere in this ledger (Amex, BNY), and it is the only causally identified estimate in the ledger. Backlog entry, not fresh news - published 23 Jan 2026 and logged now because the C ledger otherwise contains only surveys and text-mining and was missing its best-identified datapoint. Age is stated so nobody reads it as a new disclosure.
Status
DISCLOSED — realized and reported
Source
Tier C — Third-party reporting — journalism, trade press, sell-side
BIS Working Paper No. 1325 (Aldasoro, Gambacorta, Pal, Revoltella, Weiss, Wolski), 2026-01-23
Falsifier
The instrument assumes US peer adoption affects EU firm outcomes only through EU adoption; if AI adoption by US peers hits EU firms through competition or supply chains, the estimate is biased. Falsified if later vintages of EIBIS with post-2024 frontier-model adoption fail to reproduce a positive productivity effect, or if the 4% shrinks toward zero once the pre-generative-AI adopters are dropped from the sample.
Confidence
medium — Central-bank working paper with a plausible identification strategy and a large sample, but not peer reviewed, and the adoption measure is survey-reported and largely pre-dates frontier generative models - so 4% is likely an estimate of the older ML wave, not of what firms are buying in 2026.

2026-08-01

permalink →

Assessment

One main-log revenue finding, an S2, and it is the weakest kind: a growth rate and a mix percentage with no dollar behind them. Zero S3 and zero S3-CANDIDATE for the sixth consecutive run. The most useful thing found today is a St. Louis Fed corpus study that puts a number on why the log keeps coming up empty.

Sources read

  • Ford Q2 2026 (call 28 Jul, four days old - logged with its age noted): BlueCruise paid subscriptions +20% in the quarter, 50% of retail Integrated Services revenue, 1.6m total paid subscriptions, Ford Pro Intelligence 900,000+ growing >20% YoY. Logged S2. Ford said "software," never "AI"; the AI reading is this log's, not Ford's.
  • St. Louis Fed (31 Jul), ~490,000 transcripts, 5,198 firms: 95% of AI-productivity sentences are future-tense vs ~75% for non-AI productivity talk. Logged C4, counter-evidence.
  • XPO Q2 2026 (30 Jul): labour productivity +2.5 points vs a 1.5% target; AI trailer-loading pilots at +40% load quality and -50% damages. Logged C4.
  • Mastercard Q2 2026 (30 Jul): Agent Pay for Machines launched with 30+ participants; VAS revenue +18%. Miebach called agentic commerce "an entirely new class of payment use cases" and attributed no revenue at all to it.
  • GE HealthCare Q2 2026 (29 Jul): AI-enabled NPIs said to "drive higher pricing" and customers "willing to pay for it." No number.
  • Erie Indemnity Q2 2026 (31 Jul), Labcorp (30 Jul), DexCom (30 Jul), Reliance Global (30 Jul): AI described, nothing quantified against revenue.
The pattern across six runs now has independent confirmation. This log has been hand-counting the gap between AI talk and AI revenue at non-tech incumbents. The St. Louis Fed just measured the same gap mechanically across the entire US listed corpus and found AI productivity claims are roughly 5x more skewed to the future tense than ordinary productivity claims, with essentially no disconfirming commentary anywhere in 490,000 transcripts. The emptiness of this log is not a sampling artefact.

Near-misses deliberately not logged

  • Mastercard Agent Pay for Machines - the single most S3-shaped thing on this week's tape: machine-to-machine payments for AI agents buying compute, APIs and data is a payment class with no prior existence, and Mastercard says it is "the only network enabling" it. Fails the bar on result, not on shape: 30+ launch participants is adoption, and no revenue, volume or take-rate figure exists yet. This is the entry to chase in Q3.
  • Ford UEV - Farley on forthcoming ramp-to-ramp L2: "no one in that segment at that price point has anything close to that." A genuine autonomy-at-a-new-price-point claim, i.e. S3 test (d). Unshipped, unpriced, no revenue. Not loggable.
  • GE HealthCare - fails the number test. "Customers are willing to pay for it" is the right mechanism narrated without a figure attached.
  • Erie Indemnity - the instructive one. Erie disclosed that "conversions are nearly double compared to our historical online quoting platform," which is precisely the S1 metric this log hunts - and attributed it to a new online quoting platform, not to AI. Erie's two named AI assistants (claims subrogation, commercial underwriting) got only "allow our employees to spend less time gathering information." An insurer with a big conversion number declining to put AI next to it is a data point.
  • PwC 2026 AI Performance Study (13 Apr) - 74% of AI's economic value captured by 20% of organisations. Rejected: 1,217 executives self-reporting, which is a sentiment survey under the bar regardless of how good the headline is.
  • Twist Bioscience - "AI-driven orders" framing traces to the Nov 2025 FY25 call, out of window and with no AI-attributed revenue figure.
  • Reliance Global - AI agent launched, commission income down to $2.1m from $3.1m. Nothing to attribute.

Nearest dated catalysts

  • Mastercard Q3 2026 (late Oct) - first plausible window for an Agent Pay volume or revenue number. The highest-value single datapoint currently identifiable.
  • XPO Q3 2026 (late Oct) - network-wide trailer-loading rollout completes in H2; watch whether the 0.2% damage claims ratio and 79.9% LTL OR move.
  • Ford Q3 2026 (late Oct) - does Integrated Services get a dollar figure, or does the disclosure stay at growth rates and mix shares.
  • Deere Q3 FY26 (mid-Aug) - autonomy-as-recurring-revenue is the cleanest remaining industrials S3 candidate in the calendar.
  • Erie, Hippo, Ategrity Q3 - three insurers now in the log with AI capability disclosed and premium consequences not disclosed. Q3 is when that silence starts to mean something.
Ford Motor Company Automotive · US S2 — Share

On the Q2 2026 call (28 Jul 2026) CEO Jim Farley said BlueCruise "paid subscriptions in Q2 grew by 20%" and that "BlueCruise made up 50% of our retail Integrated Services revenue"; Ford reported 1.6m total paid subscriptions, including 900,000+ Ford Pro Intelligence subscriptions "growing over 20% year-over-year".

Mechanism
BlueCruise is a machine-learning-based hands-free highway driving system sold as a standalone subscription ($49.99/month, $495/year, $2,495 one-time) on top of the vehicle. Revenue is the subscription itself, attached at or after point of sale by dealers; Farley noted "our dealers are getting better at selling" it. Ford Pro Intelligence is the equivalent telematics/fleet-software subscription on the commercial side.
Why this shape
S2, not S3. Test (a) counterfactual purchase: a buyer wanting hands-free highway driving could buy GM's Super Cruise or Tesla FSD, so the demand pre-exists and a competitor lost this sale. Test (b) prior alternative: a paid competitor product, not doing-without. Test (c) capability gate: FAILS decisively - BlueCruise has shipped since 2021 and Super Cruise since 2017; nothing here needs a capability that did not exist 24 months ago. Test (d) newly economic: no evidence offered. Within Ford's OWN catalog there is no predecessor SKU, which is why this reads S3-ish on first pass - that is exactly the misclassification METHOD warns about, since the test is industry-level counterfactual, not catalog-level novelty. LOAD-BEARING CAVEAT: Ford framed this as "software," not "AI," on the call; the AI characterisation is the log's, on the grounds that hands-free autonomy is a neural-network product. Discount accordingly. Also note what is NOT disclosed: no absolute dollar of Integrated Services revenue. The only absolute figure Farley gave was forward-looking - "we could absolutely see this business, the Integrated Services, being a half point of margin for the company" - which is PROJECTED, not realized.
Status

Ford Motor Q2 2026 earnings call, 2026-07-28
Falsifier
If Q3 or Q4 2026 drops the paid-subscription growth rate and the BlueCruise share of retail Integrated Services revenue from disclosure, or if Integrated Services never appears as a named contributor in Ford's margin bridge, the 'half point of margin' was narrative. A second falsifier: subscription COUNT growth continuing while Ford still refuses to state Integrated Services revenue in dollars would suggest the base is too small to be material.
